Global existence of suitable weak solutions to the 3D chemotaxis-Navier-Stokes equations
Xiaomeng Chen, Shuai Li, Lili Wang, Wendong Wang

Abstract
. In 2004, Dombrowski et al. showed that suspensions of aerobic bacteria often develop flows from the interplay of chemotaxis and buoyancy, which is described as the chemotaxis-Navier-Stokes model, and they observed self-concentration occurs as a turbulence by exhibiting transient, reconstituting, high-speed jets, which entrains nearby fluid to produce paired, oppositely signed vortices. In order to investigate the properties of these vortices (singular points), one approach is to follow the partial regularity theory of Caffarelli-Kohn-Nirenberg to study the singularity properties of suitable weak solutions. In this paper, we established the existence of suitable weak solution for the three dimensional chemotaxis-Navier-Stokes equations, where the main difficulty is to establish appropriate local energy inequalities.



Keywords:
chemotaxis-Navier-Stokes, suitable weak solution, local energy inequalities

1.
Introduction

We consider the following model describing the dynamics of oxygen,
swimming bacteria, and viscous incompressible fluids, which was proposed by Tuval et al.
[
29
]
in
Q_{T}=\mathbb{R}^{3}\times(0,T)
:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{llll}\displaystyle\partial_{t}n+u\cdot\nabla n-\Delta n=-\nabla\cdot(\chi(c)n\nabla c),\\
\displaystyle\partial_{t}c+u\cdot\nabla c-\Delta c=-\kappa(c)n,\\
\displaystyle\partial_{t}u+\mu u\cdot\nabla u-\Delta u+\nabla P=-n\nabla\phi,\\
\displaystyle\nabla\cdot u=0,\end{array}\right.

where
c(x,t):Q_{T}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{+}
,
n(x,t):Q_{T}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{+}
,
u(x,t):Q_{T}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{3}
and
P(x,t):Q_{T}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
denote the oxygen concentration, cell concentration, the fluid velocity and the associated pressure, respectively. Moreover, the
gravitational potential
\phi
, the chemotactic sensitivity
\chi(c)\geq 0
and the per-capita oxygen
consumption rate
\kappa(c)\geq 0
are supposed to be sufficiently smooth given functions. Here
\mu=1
or
0
, and the system of (
1
) is reduced to the chemotaxis-Stokes model for
\mu=0.

As Dombrowski et al. observed in
[
7
]
(see also
[
29
]
):
local concentration for suspensions of aerobic bacteria leads to a jet descending faster
than its surroundings, which entrains nearby fluid to
produce paired, oppositely signed vortices. Thus this system has very strong physical background. It is necessary to study the existence of solutions and the singularity properties of weak solutions.
Recently, Chen-Li-Wang
[
5
]
studied partial regularity of strong solutions to the simplified 3D chemotaxis-Navier-Stokes equations (
\kappa(c)=c
,
\chi(c)=1
) at the first blow-up time. However, it’s still unknown whether there exists a suitable weak solution for such system. Here we consider the existence of general chemotaxis-Navier-Stokes equations, and discuss partial regularity of these weak solutions in a forthcoming paper. About suitable weak solutions of Navier-Stokes equations, it was started by Scheffer in
[
22
,
23
,
24
]
, and later Caffarelli-Kohn-Nirenberg
[
2
]
showed that the set
\mathcal{S}
of possible interior singular points of a suitable weak solution is one-dimensional parabolic Hausdorff measure zero.
The suitable weak solution is better than Leray-Hopf weak solution introduced by Leray in

[
17
]
and if the local strong solution blows up, then the solution may be continued as a
suitable weak solution (see Proposition 30.1 in
[
16
]
).
More references on simplified proofs and improvements, we refer to Lin
[
19
]
, Ladyzhenskaya-Seregin
[
15
]
, Tian-Xin
[
28
]
, Seregin
[
25
]
, Gustafson-Kang-Tsai
[
11
]
, Vasseur
[
31
]
and the references therein.

Due to the significance of the biological background (see
[
7
]
,
[
29
]
), the model could be used to predict the
large-scale bioconvection affecting clearly the overall oxygen consumption in the
above experiments (see, for example,
[
21
]
). Many mathematicians have studied this model and made much progress,
and we just mention some related works for the result.
Firstly, for the existence of weak solutions, global classical solutions near constant steady states are constructed for the full
chemotaxis-Navier-Stokes system by Duan-Lorz-Markowich in
[
9
]
. In
[
21
]
, for the case of bounded domain of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
with
n=2,3
, the local existence of weak solutions for problem (
1
) is obtained by Lorz. Later, Winkler proved the existence of global weak solution in
[
36
]
by assuming that

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\kappa}{\chi}\right)^{\prime}>0,\quad\left(\frac{\kappa}{\chi}\right)^{\prime\prime}\leq 0,\quad\left({\kappa}{\chi}\right)^{\prime}\geq 0.

By assuming
\chi^{\prime},\kappa^{\prime}\geq 0
and
\kappa(0)=0
, local well-posed results and blow-up criteria were established by Chae-Kang-Lee in
[
3
]
. In
[
4
]
, under the conditions of
\chi,\kappa\in C^{m},\kappa(0)=0
,
\chi,\kappa,\chi^{{}^{\prime}},\kappa^{{}^{\prime}}\geq 0
and the smallness of
\|c_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}
, Chae-Kang-Lee proved that classical solution of (
1
) exists globally.
Recently, Winkler proved the global existence of weak solutions of the system (
1
) in bounded domain with large initial data, and obtained much better a priori estimates such as
\frac{|\nabla c|^{4}}{c^{3}}\in L^{1}
in
[
37
]
. Secondly, for the two-dimensional system of (
1
), the system is better understood. Liu and Lorz
[
20
]
proved the global existence of weak solutions to the two-dimensional system of (
1
) for arbitrarily large initial data, under the assumptions on
\chi
and
f
made in
[
9
]
. See also
[
18
,
8
,
33
,
34
,
13
,
35
]
and the references therein.
As for the case of the chemotaxis-Navier-Stokes system with a nonlinear diffusion, that means
\Delta n
is replaced by
\Delta n^{m}
, there are also many works. one can refer to
[
10
,
26
,
27
,
6
,
39
]
and so on.

As Winkler said in
[
37
]
,
“For the full three-dimensional chemotaxis-Navier-Stokes system, even at the very basic level of global existence in generalized solution frameworks, a satisfactory solution theory is entirely lacking.”
For the simplified model of the case
\kappa(c)=c
and
\chi(c)=1
, the three dimensional chemotaxis-Navier-Stokes system (
1
) is reduced to

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{llll}\displaystyle\partial_{t}n+u\cdot\nabla n-\Delta n=-\nabla\cdot(n\nabla c),\\
\displaystyle\partial_{t}c+u\cdot\nabla c-\Delta c=-cn,\\
\displaystyle\partial_{t}u+u\cdot\nabla u-\Delta u+\nabla p=-n\nabla\phi,~~\nabla\cdot u=0.\\
\end{array}\right.

Furthermore, global weak solutions of Leray-Hopf type for this system was obtained in 2D and 3D by Zhang-Zheng
[
38
]
, He-Zhang
[
12
]
, and Kang-Lee-Winkler
[
14
]
, respectively, where they established a priori estimate

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}(t)+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{V}(t)d\tau\leq Ce^{Ct},

(1.10)

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}=\|n\|_{L^{1}\cap L\log L}+\|\nabla\sqrt{c}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|u\|_{L^{2}}^{2},

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}=\|\nabla\sqrt{n+1}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\Delta\sqrt{c}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\sqrt{c})^{-2}|\nabla\sqrt{c}|^{4}dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}n|\nabla\sqrt{c}|^{2}dx,

where
d=2,3
and denote by
L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})=L^{p}
for simplicity.
However, up to now more information about these weak solutions is still not known, especially for the interior singular vortices as described in
[
7
]
or the self-organized generation of a persistent hydrodynamic vortex that traps cells near the contact line(see
[
29
]
).

Our main objective of this paper is to present global existence of suitable weak soultion for (
1
) in three dimensions.
Assume that

\displaystyle\chi(s)\in C^{2}(\overline{\mathbb{R^{+}}});~~~~~\chi(s)\geq 0;

(1.11)

\displaystyle\kappa(s)\in C^{2}(\overline{\mathbb{R^{+}}}),~~~~\kappa(0)=0,~~~~\kappa^{\prime}(s)\geq 0,~~~~\kappa^{\prime\prime}(s)\geq 0;

(1.12)

and

\displaystyle\kappa(s)=\Theta_{0}s\chi(s),

(1.13)

where
\Theta_{0}>0
is a positive absolute constant.

Definition 1.1
.

A triplet
(n,c,u)
is called a suitable weak solution of the system (
1
) if the following holds:

(i). For any bounded domain
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{3}
,

n,n\ln n\in L^{\infty}_{\rm loc}([0,+\infty);L^{1}(\Omega))
,
\nabla\sqrt{n}\in L^{2}_{\rm loc}([0,+\infty);L^{2}(\Omega))
,

\nabla\sqrt{c}\in L^{\infty}_{\rm loc}([0,+\infty);L^{2}(\Omega))\cap L^{2}_{\rm loc}([0,+\infty);H^{1}(\Omega))
,

u\in L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}([0,+\infty);L^{2}(\Omega))\cap L^{2}_{\rm loc}([0,+\infty);H^{1}(\Omega))
,
P\in L^{\frac{3}{2}}_{\rm loc}([0,+\infty);L^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega))
;

(ii).
(n,c,u)
solves (
1
) in the sense of distributions;

(iii).
(n,c,u)
satisfies energy inequality

\displaystyle||u||_{L^{2}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}||\nabla u(t)||_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle+~\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(n+1)\ln(n+1)(\cdot,t)+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla\sqrt{n+1}|^{2}

\displaystyle+~\frac{2}{\Theta_{0}}||\nabla\sqrt{c}||_{L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{4}{3\Theta_{0}}\int_{0}^{t}||\nabla^{2}\sqrt{c}||_{L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{1}{3\Theta_{0}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\sqrt{c})^{-2}|\nabla\sqrt{c}|^{4}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^{\infty}},\|n_{0}\|_{L^{1}},\|c_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}},\|c_{0}\|_{L^{1}},\|u_{0}\|_{L^{2}},\|(n_{0}+1)\ln(n_{0}+1)\|_{L^{1}},\|\nabla\sqrt{c_{0}}\|_{L^{2}})(1+t);

(iv).
(n,c,u)
satisfies local energy inequality

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}(n\ln(n)\psi)(\cdot,t)+4\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|\nabla\sqrt{n}|^{2}\psi+\frac{2}{\Theta_{0}}\int_{\Omega}(|\nabla\sqrt{c}|^{2}\psi)(\cdot,t)

\displaystyle+\frac{4}{3\Theta_{0}}\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|\Delta\sqrt{c}|^{2}\psi+\frac{18}{\Theta_{0}}\|{c_{0}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{\Omega}(|u|^{2})(\cdot,t)\psi

\displaystyle+\frac{18}{\Theta_{0}}\|{c_{0}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|\nabla u|^{2}\psi+\frac{2}{3\Theta_{0}}\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}(\sqrt{c})^{-2}|\nabla\sqrt{c}|^{4}\psi

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}n\ln(n)(\partial_{t}\psi+\Delta\psi)+\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}n\ln(n)u\cdot\nabla\psi

\displaystyle+\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}n\chi(c)\nabla c\cdot\nabla\psi+\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}n\ln n\chi(c)\nabla c\cdot\nabla\psi

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{\Theta_{0}}\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|\nabla\sqrt{c}|^{2}(\partial_{t}\psi+\Delta\psi)+\frac{2}{\Theta_{0}}\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|\nabla\sqrt{c}|^{2}u\cdot\nabla\psi

\displaystyle+\frac{18}{\Theta_{0}}||c_{0}||_{L^{\infty}}\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|u|^{2}\left(\partial_{t}\psi+\Delta\psi\right)+\frac{18\mu}{\Theta_{0}}||c_{0}||_{L^{\infty}}\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|u|^{2}u\cdot\nabla\psi

\displaystyle+\frac{36}{\Theta_{0}}||c_{0}||_{L^{\infty}}\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}(P-\bar{P})u\cdot\nabla\psi-\frac{36}{\Theta_{0}}||c_{0}||_{L^{\infty}}\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}n\nabla\phi\cdot u\psi,

where
\psi\geq 0
and vanishes in the parabolic boundary of
(0,t)\times\Omega.

Our main results are as follows.

Theorem 1.2
.

Assume that the initial data
(n_{0},c_{0},u_{0})
satisfies

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{llll}\displaystyle n_{0}\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}),\quad(n_{0}+1)\ln(n_{0}+1)\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}),\quad u_{0}\in L^{2}_{\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{3});\\
\displaystyle\nabla\sqrt{c_{0}}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}),\quad c_{0}\in L^{1}\cap L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3});\\
\displaystyle n_{0}\geq 0,\quad c_{0}\geq 0.\\
\end{array}\right.

Moreover,
\nabla\phi\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
,
\kappa
and
\chi
satisfy
(
1.11
)
,
(
1.12
)
and
\eqref{equ:chi kappa}.

Then there exists a global suitable weak solution of the system (
1
). Moreover, for the chemotaxis-Stokes model of
\mu=0
, assume that
(-\triangle)^{\frac{1}{4}}u_{0}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
additionally, and one can get a new a priori estimate of the velocity:

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|(-\Delta)^{\frac{1}{4}}u(\cdot,t)|^{2}dx+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla(-\Delta)^{\frac{1}{4}}u|^{2}dxds

(1.18)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^{\infty}},\|n_{0}\|_{L^{1}},\|c_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}\cap L^{1}},\|u_{0}\|_{L^{2}},\|(n_{0}+1)\ln(n_{0}+1)\|_{L^{1}},\|\nabla\sqrt{c_{0}}\|_{L^{2}})(1+t).

Remark 1.3
.

(i) Note that the right hand side term of
(\ref{eq:GKS})_{1}
is supper-critical, which is cancelled by the right hand term of
c
’s equation. When
(
1.13
)
fails, it is still unknown whether the system of (
1
) has a suitable weak solution or even a Leray-Hopf weak solution.

(ii) For
\mu=1
, Chae-Kang-Lee in
[
3
]
constructed a global weak solution by assuming
\chi=\mu_{0}\kappa
and using the iterative scheme developed in
[
9
]
, where they obtained the estimates of
\|\nabla c\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}
and
\|\nabla^{2}c\|_{L^{2}((0,t)\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}
. Here we introduce a mollifier dealing with the nonlinear terms of
n,u
, and obtain the estimate of
\|({\sqrt{c}})^{-1}|\nabla\sqrt{c}|^{2}\|_{L^{2}((0,t)\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}
by assuming (
1.13
) for the equation of
\sqrt{c}
, which seems to be important in the local energy inequality.
Compared with global weak solutions of the simplified model in
[
12
]
by He-Zhang and
[
14
]
by Kang-Lee-Winkler, we consider the more general model, and obtained local energy inequality by proving the strong convergence of
n\ln n
in
L^{\frac{3}{2}}((0,t)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}))
. Moreover, the uniform a priori energy estimates is linear with respect to the time
t
, which is slower than the exponential growth (
1.10
)
in
[
12
]

or
[
14
]
.

(iii) For
\mu=0
, we get a better a priori estimate of the velocity in (
1.18
), and it’s still unknown whether one can get higher regularity estimates for
n
and
c
. If
\chi(c)
is replaced by
S(n,c)\geq 0
and
S(n,c)\leq C(1+n)^{-\alpha}
with
\alpha>0
, referring to the known results
[
32
]
, the solution is globally bounded.

The proof of Theorem
1.2
is based on the following global well-posedness result of the following regularized system:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{llll}\displaystyle\partial_{t}n^{\varepsilon,\tau}+(u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla n^{\varepsilon,\tau}-\Delta n^{\varepsilon,\tau}=-\nabla\cdot\left(\frac{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}}{1+\tau n^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\chi(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})\right),\\
\displaystyle\partial_{t}c^{\varepsilon,\tau}+u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}-\Delta c^{\varepsilon,\tau}=-\frac{1}{\tau}\ln(1+\tau n^{\varepsilon,\tau})\kappa(c^{\varepsilon,\tau}),\\
\displaystyle\partial_{t}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}+\mu(u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\tau}-\Delta u^{\varepsilon,\tau}+\nabla P^{\varepsilon,\tau}=-(n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\nabla\phi)\ast\rho^{\varepsilon},\\
\displaystyle\nabla\cdot u^{\varepsilon,\tau}=0,\\
\displaystyle n^{\varepsilon}(x,0)=n^{\varepsilon}_{0}(x),\quad c^{\varepsilon}(x,0)=c^{\varepsilon}_{0}(x),\quad u^{\varepsilon}(x,0)=u^{\varepsilon}_{0}(x),\\
\end{array}\right.

where
\rho^{\varepsilon}
is a standard mollifier,
\tau>0
is a sufficiently small constant, and the initial value
n_{0}^{\varepsilon}

,
c_{0}^{\varepsilon}
and
u_{0}^{\varepsilon}
are defined by
n_{0}^{\varepsilon}(x)=n_{0}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon}
,
c^{\varepsilon}_{0}(x)=\left(\sqrt{c_{0}}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon}\right)^{2}
and
u^{\varepsilon}_{0}(x)=u_{0}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon}
respectively.

The existence of strong solutions is established as follows.

Theorem 1.4
.

Let
\nabla\phi\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
and the initial value

(n_{0}^{\varepsilon},c_{0}^{\varepsilon},\nabla c_{0}^{\varepsilon},u_{0}^{\varepsilon})\in H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}),

n_{0}^{\varepsilon},c_{0}^{\varepsilon}\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}),

\chi,\kappa
satisfy
(
1.11
)
and
(
1.12
)
. Assume
n_{0}^{\varepsilon}(x)\geq 0
and
c_{0}^{\varepsilon}(x)\geq 0
for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{3}.
Then there exists a global strong solution
(n^{\varepsilon,\tau},c^{\varepsilon,\tau},u^{\varepsilon,\tau})
of system (
1
), which satisfies

(i)

\displaystyle~~(n^{\varepsilon,\tau},c^{\varepsilon,\tau},\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau},u^{\varepsilon,\tau})\in C([0,T);H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))\cap L^{2}((0,T);H^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{3}));

and

\displaystyle\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau},c^{\varepsilon,\tau},\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau},u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{2}_{x}}^{2}+\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau},c^{\varepsilon,\tau},\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau},u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{2}_{x}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left(\varepsilon,\tau,\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^{\infty}},\|\chi\|_{0},\|\kappa\|_{0},\|n_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{1}},\|E_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{2}}\right)(T+1)^{54},

for any
t\in(0,T),
where

\displaystyle\|\chi\|_{0}=\|\chi\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\|c_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}})}+\|\chi^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\|c_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}})}+\|\chi^{\prime\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\|c_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}})},

\displaystyle\|\kappa\|_{0}=\|\kappa\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\|c\|_{L^{\infty}})}+\|\kappa^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\|c_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}})}+\|\kappa^{\prime\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\|c_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}})}.

(ii)
\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}(\cdot,t)\|_{L^{1}}\leq\|n^{\varepsilon}_{0}\|_{L^{1}},\quad\|c^{\varepsilon,\tau}(\cdot,t)\|_{L^{1}}\leq\|c^{\varepsilon}_{0}\|_{L^{1}}.

Remark 1.5
.

Indeed, we can consider the following system:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{llll}\displaystyle\partial_{t}n^{\varepsilon,\tau}+(u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla n^{\varepsilon,\tau}-\Delta n^{\varepsilon,\tau}=-\nabla\cdot\left(\frac{n_{+}^{\varepsilon,\tau}}{1+\tau n_{+}^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\chi(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})\right),\\
\displaystyle\partial_{t}c^{\varepsilon,\tau}+u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}-\Delta c^{\varepsilon,\tau}=-\frac{1}{\tau}\ln(1+\tau n_{+}^{\varepsilon,\tau})\kappa(c^{\varepsilon,\tau}),\\
\displaystyle\partial_{t}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}+(u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\tau}-\Delta u^{\varepsilon,\tau}+\nabla P^{\varepsilon,\tau}=-(n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\nabla\phi)\ast\rho^{\varepsilon},\\
\displaystyle\nabla\cdot u^{\varepsilon,\tau}=0,\\
\displaystyle n^{\varepsilon}(x,0)=n^{\varepsilon}_{0}(x),\quad c^{\varepsilon}(x,0)=c^{\varepsilon}_{0}(x),\quad u^{\varepsilon}(x,0)=u^{\varepsilon}_{0}(x),\\
\end{array}\right.

where
n_{+}^{\varepsilon,\tau}=\max\{0,n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\}.
By Banach fixed theorem there exists a classical solution of (
1.5
) over
(0,T)
for a small
T
, and one can prove
n_{-}^{\varepsilon,\tau}=0
over
(0,T),
thus the system (
1.5
) is reduced to (
1
). Hence it suffices to consider the system of (
1
).

Remark 1.6
.

It is worth noting that we did not use a smoothing operator for the nonlinear term in the equation of
c
. One reason is that its regularity can be drived from the equations of
u
and
n
. Another key reason is that it comes from the elimination estimation of local energy inequalities. That is to say, the bad term
\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|\nabla(u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})|^{2}\psi
from
(u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}
seems to be not cancelled by the term
\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{2}\psi
(see the estimate of
J_{2}
and (
4.11
)).

Throughout this article,
C
denotes an absolute constant independent of
(n,c,u)
and may be different from line to line.
Denote by
L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})=L^{p}
and
W^{m,p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})=W^{m,p}
the standard Lebesgue spaces and Sobolev spaces, respectively. In the case
p=2,
we denote
W^{m,2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})=H^{m}(\mathbb{R}^{3})=H^{m}.

The rest of the paper is organized as follows. Section 2 is devoted to obtain the global existence of strong solutions to the regularized system (
1
). In Section 3, we prove uniform estimates independent of
\varepsilon
and
\tau
for the regularized system (
1
). Local energy inequality (
4.8
) is established in Section 4. Besides, we derive convergence of
n^{\varepsilon,\tau}
,
u^{\varepsilon,\tau}
and
c^{\varepsilon,\tau}
respectively by Aubin-Lions Lemma in Section 5. Section 6 is to prove
convergence of local energy inequality when
\tau\rightarrow 0
and
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
, respectively. The proof of Theorem
1.2
is in Section 7. Some technical lemmas are shown in the Appendix.

2.
Globally well-posedness theorem to the regularized system (
1
)

This section is devoted to proving the global well-posedness of the following regularized system (
1
).

Proof of Theorem
1.4
.
Let
R=2\left(\|n_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{2}}+\|c_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{3}}+\|u_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{2}}\right)<\infty

and
T\in(0,1)
to be decided, and we introduce the Banach space as follows.

X:=L^{\infty}[(0,T);\{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\cap W^{1,2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\}\times\{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\cap W^{2,2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\}\times W^{2,2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})],

along with its closed subset

\displaystyle S

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\{(n^{\varepsilon,\tau},c^{\varepsilon,\tau},u^{\varepsilon,\tau})\in X\big|\sup_{t\in(0,T)}\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}(\cdot,t)\|_{L^{\infty}}+\sup_{t\in(0,T)}\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}(\cdot,t)\|_{W^{1,2}}

(2.1)

\displaystyle+\sup_{t\in(0,T)}\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}(\cdot,t)\|_{L^{\infty}}+\sup_{t\in(0,T)}\|c^{\varepsilon,\tau}(\cdot,t)\|_{L^{2}}+\sup_{t\in(0,T)}\|\nabla^{2}c^{\varepsilon,\tau}(\cdot,t)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\sup_{t\in(0,T)}\|u^{\varepsilon,\tau}(\cdot,t)\|_{L^{2}}+\sup_{t\in(0,T)}\|\nabla^{2}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}(\cdot,t)\|_{L^{2}}\leq R\}.

For
(n^{\varepsilon,\tau},c^{\varepsilon,\tau},u^{\varepsilon,\tau})\in S
and
t\in(0,T),
we let

\displaystyle\Phi(n^{\varepsilon,\tau},c^{\varepsilon,\tau},u^{\varepsilon,\tau})(\cdot,t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{llll}\Phi_{1}(n^{\varepsilon,\tau},c^{\varepsilon,\tau},u^{\varepsilon,\tau})(\cdot,t)\\
\Phi_{2}(n^{\varepsilon,\tau},c^{\varepsilon,\tau},u^{\varepsilon,\tau})(\cdot,t)\\
\Phi_{3}(n^{\varepsilon,\tau},c^{\varepsilon,\tau},u^{\varepsilon,\tau})(\cdot,t)\\
\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{llll}e^{t\Delta}n_{0}^{\varepsilon,\tau}-\int_{0}^{t}e^{(t-s)\Delta}\big\{\nabla\cdot\left(\frac{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}}{1+\tau n^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\chi(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})\right)+(u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\big\}(\cdot,s)ds\\
e^{t\Delta}c_{0}^{\varepsilon,\tau}-\int_{0}^{t}e^{(t-s)\Delta}\big\{\frac{1}{\tau}\ln(1+\tau n^{\varepsilon,\tau})\kappa(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})+u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\big\}(\cdot,s)ds\\
e^{t\Delta}u_{0}^{\varepsilon,\tau}-\int_{0}^{t}e^{(t-s)\Delta}\mathcal{P}\big\{\mu(u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\tau}+(n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\nabla\phi)\ast\rho^{\varepsilon}\big\}(\cdot,s)ds\\
\end{array}\right),

where
(e^{t\Delta})_{t\geq 0}

and
\mathcal{P}
denote the heat semigroup
and
the Leray projection in
L^{2}
, respectively. The proof is divided into five steps as follows.

Step I.
\Phi
is a mapping from
S
to
S
.

Estimation of
\Phi_{1}
.

Noting that
\|e^{t\Delta}n_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq\|n_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}}
due to the maximum principle and heat kernel estimates of Lemma
8.18
in the appendix, we have

\displaystyle\|\Phi_{1}(n^{\varepsilon,\tau},c^{\varepsilon,\tau},u^{\varepsilon,\tau})(\cdot,t)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|e^{t\Delta}n_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}}+C\int_{0}^{t}\left\|e^{-(t-s)\Delta}\nabla\cdot\left(\frac{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}}{1+\tau n^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\chi(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})+(u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\right)\right\|_{L^{\infty}}ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|n_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}}+C\int_{0}^{t}(t-s)^{-\frac{7}{8}}\left\|(\frac{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}}{1+\tau n^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\chi(c^{\varepsilon,\tau}))+(u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\right\|_{L^{4}}ds.

It follows from (
2.1
) that
\|c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\|\nabla^{2}c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{3/4}\|c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{1/4}\leq CR
due to embedding inequality, then by (
1.11
) and (
1.12
) we know

\displaystyle\|\chi(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C(R),~~\|\chi^{\prime}(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C(R);

\displaystyle\|\kappa(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C(R),~~\|\kappa^{\prime}(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C(R).

(2.2)

Moreover, using

\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{4}}\leq C\|c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{8}}\|\nabla^{2}c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{7}{8}},

(2.3)

\|u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{4}}\leq C\|u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{5}{8}}\|\nabla^{2}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{3}{8}},

(2.4)

Young inequality and (
2
), we get

\displaystyle\|\Phi_{1}(n^{\varepsilon,\tau},c^{\varepsilon,\tau},u^{\varepsilon,\tau})(\cdot,t)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|n_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}}+C\int_{0}^{t}(t-s)^{-\frac{7}{8}}\left\{\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{4}}\|\chi(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{4}}\|\rho^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{1}}\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\infty}}\right\}ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|n_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}}+C(R)T^{\frac{1}{8}}\quad\quad~~{\rm{for~~all}}~~t\in(0,T).

Direct calculation and (
2.1
) yield that

\displaystyle\|\Phi_{1}(n^{\varepsilon,\tau},c^{\varepsilon,\tau},u^{\varepsilon,\tau})(\cdot,t)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|n_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}+C\int_{0}^{t}(t-s)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left\|\frac{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}}{1+\tau n^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\chi(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})+(u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\right\|_{L^{2}}ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|n_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}+C\int_{0}^{t}(t-s)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left(\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\chi(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\rho^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{1}}\|\nabla n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}\right)ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|n_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}+C(R)T^{\frac{1}{2}}\quad\quad~~{\rm{for~~all}}~~t\in(0,T).

By (
2.3
) and

\|u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\|u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{4}}\|\nabla^{2}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{3}{4}},

(2.5)

noting that
\nabla\cdot u^{\varepsilon,\tau}=0,
we have

\displaystyle\|\nabla\Phi_{1}(n^{\varepsilon,\tau},c^{\varepsilon,\tau},u^{\varepsilon,\tau})(\cdot,t)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\nabla n_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}+\int_{0}^{t}\left\|\nabla e^{(t-s)\Delta}\nabla\cdot\left(\frac{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}}{1+\tau n^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\cdot\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\chi(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})+(u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}}ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\nabla n_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}+C\int_{0}^{t}(t-s)^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\|\nabla n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\chi(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle+~\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{\infty}\|\nabla^{2}c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}\|\chi(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{4}}^{2}\|\chi^{\prime}(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle+~\|u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\rho^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{1}}\|\nabla n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}})ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\nabla n_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}+C(R)T^{\frac{1}{2}}\quad\quad~~{\rm{for~~all}}~~t\in(0,T).

Estimation of
\Phi_{2}
.

Similar to the estimate of
\|\Phi_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}},
note that for any
n^{\varepsilon,\tau}>0,

\tau>0,

\displaystyle\ln(1+\tau n^{\varepsilon,\tau})\leq\tau n^{\varepsilon,\tau}.

(2.6)

Besides, by

\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{4}}\leq C\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{4}}\|\nabla n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{3}{4}},

(2.7)

(
2.3
) and (
2.5
), we get

\displaystyle\|\nabla\Phi_{2}(n^{\varepsilon,\tau},c^{\varepsilon,\tau},u^{\varepsilon,\tau})(\cdot,t)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\nabla e^{t\Delta}c_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\int_{0}^{t}\left\|\nabla e^{(t-s)\Delta}\left\{\frac{1}{\tau}\ln(1+\tau n^{\varepsilon,\tau})\kappa(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})+u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\right\}(\cdot,s)\right\|_{L^{\infty}}ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\nabla c_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}}+C\int_{0}^{t}(t-s)^{-\frac{7}{8}}\left\|\frac{1}{\tau}\ln(1+\tau n^{\varepsilon,\tau})\kappa(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})+u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\right\|_{L^{4}}ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\nabla c_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}}+C\int_{0}^{t}(t-s)^{-\frac{7}{8}}\left\{\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{4}}\|\kappa(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{4}}\right\}ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\nabla c_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}}+C(R)T^{\frac{1}{8}}\quad\quad~~{\rm{for~~all}}~~t\in(0,T).

By (
2.3
) and (
2.4
), we have

\displaystyle\|\Phi_{2}(n^{\varepsilon,\tau},c^{\varepsilon,\tau},u^{\varepsilon,\tau})(\cdot,t)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|e^{t\Delta}c_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}+\int_{0}^{t}\left\|e^{(t-s)\Delta}\left\{\frac{1}{\tau}\ln(1+\tau n^{\varepsilon,\tau})\kappa(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})+u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\right\}(\cdot,s)\right\|_{L^{2}}ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|c_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}+C\int_{0}^{t}\left(\|\frac{\ln(1+\tau n^{\varepsilon,\tau})}{\tau}\|_{L^{2}}\|\kappa(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{4}}\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{4}}\right)ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|c_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}+C(R)T\quad\quad~~{\rm{for~~all}}~~t\in(0,T).

By (
2.5
) and

\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\|u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla^{2}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}};

(2.8)

there holds

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{2}\Phi_{2}(n^{\varepsilon,\tau},c^{\varepsilon,\tau},u^{\varepsilon,\tau})(\cdot,t)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{2}c_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}+C\int_{0}^{t}(t-s)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left\|\frac{\nabla n^{\varepsilon,\tau}}{1+\tau n^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\kappa(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})+\frac{\ln(1+\tau n^{\varepsilon,\tau})}{\tau}\kappa^{\prime}(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+C\int_{0}^{t}(t-s)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}+u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot\nabla^{2}c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\right\|_{L^{2}}ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{2}c_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}+C(\tau)\int_{0}^{t}(t-s)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left\{\|\nabla n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}\|\kappa(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}\|\kappa^{\prime}(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\infty}}\right\}ds

\displaystyle+C(\tau)\int_{0}^{t}(t-s)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left\{\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla^{2}c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}\right\}ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{2}c_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}+C(R)T^{\frac{1}{2}}\quad\quad~~{\rm{for~~all}}~~t\in(0,T).

Estimation of
\Phi_{3}
.

By (
2.4
) and

\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{4}}\leq C\|u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{8}}\|\nabla^{2}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{7}{8}},

(2.9)

we have

\displaystyle\|\Phi_{3}(n^{\varepsilon,\tau},c^{\varepsilon,\tau},u^{\varepsilon,\tau})(\cdot,t)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|e^{t\Delta}u_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}+\int_{0}^{t}\left\|e^{(t-s)\Delta}\mathcal{P}\left\{\mu(u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\tau}+(n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\nabla\phi)\ast\rho^{\varepsilon}\right\}(\cdot,s)\right\|_{L^{2}}ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|u_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}+C\int_{0}^{t}\big(\|u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{4}}\|\rho^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{1}}\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{4}}+\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\rho^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{1}}\big)ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|u_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}+C(R)T\quad\quad~~{\rm{for~~all}}~~t\in(0,T).

By (
2.9
) and (
2.5
), we achieve

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{2}\Phi_{3}(n^{\varepsilon,\tau},c^{\varepsilon,\tau},u^{\varepsilon,\tau})(\cdot,t)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{2}e^{-tA}u_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}+\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla e^{-(t-s)A}\nabla\mathcal{P}\big\{\mu(u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\tau}+(n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\nabla\phi)\ast\rho^{\varepsilon}\big\}(\cdot,s)\|_{L^{2}}ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{2}u_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}+C\int_{0}^{t}(t-s)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big(\|(\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\tau}+(u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla^{2}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}

\displaystyle+\nabla(n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\nabla\phi)\ast\rho^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}\big)ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{2}u_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}+C\int_{0}^{t}(t-s)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big(\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{4}}^{2}\|\rho^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{1}}+\|u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\rho^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{1}}\|\nabla^{2}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla\rho^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{1}}\big)ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{2}u_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}+C(R,\varepsilon)T^{\frac{1}{2}}\quad\quad~~{\rm{for~~all}}~~t\in(0,T).

Combining the estimates of
\Phi_{1}
,
\Phi_{2}
and
\Phi_{3}
, we get
\Phi
is a mapping from
S
to
S
.

Step II. To show
\Phi
acts as a contraction on
S.

Furthermore, using the same idea, we can obtain the following estimates for
(n_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau},c_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau},u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau})
,
(n_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau},c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau},u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau})\in S
.

Estimates of
n^{\varepsilon,\tau}
.
For
L^{2}
norm of
n^{\varepsilon,\tau},
we have

\displaystyle\|\Phi_{1}(n_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau},c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau},u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau})-\Phi_{1}(n_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau},c_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau},u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau})(\cdot,t)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big\|\int_{0}^{t}e^{(t-s)\Delta}\big\{\nabla\cdot[\frac{n_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}}{1+\tau n_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\nabla c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\chi(c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau})+(u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot n_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}

\displaystyle-\frac{n_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}}{1+\tau n_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\nabla c_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\chi(c_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau})-(u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot n_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}]\big\}ds\big\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{0}^{t}(t-s)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|\frac{n_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}}{1+\tau n_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\nabla c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\chi(c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau})+(u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot n_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}

\displaystyle-\frac{n_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}}{1+\tau n_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\nabla c_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\chi(c_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau})-(u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot n_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(R)T^{\frac{1}{2}}\|(n_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau},c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau},u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau})-(n_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau},c_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau},u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau})\|_{S},

where we used
|\chi(c_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau})-\chi(c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau})|\leq\chi^{\prime}(R)|c_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}-c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}|.

For
L^{\infty}
norm of
n^{\varepsilon,\tau}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\Phi_{1}(n_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau},c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau},u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau})(\cdot,t)-\Phi_{1}(n_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau},c_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau},u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau})(\cdot,t)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|\int_{0}^{t}e^{(t-s)\Delta}\big\{\nabla\cdot\left(\frac{n_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}}{1+\tau n_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\nabla c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\chi(c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau})+(u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot n_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\right)

\displaystyle-~\nabla\cdot\left(\frac{n_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}}{1+\tau n_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\nabla c_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\chi(c_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau})+(u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot n_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\right)\big\}\|_{L^{\infty}}ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(R)T^{\frac{1}{8}}\|(n_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau},c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau},u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau})-(n_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau},c_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau},u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau})\|_{S}.

Finally, for
{\dot{W}^{1,2}}
norm about
n^{\varepsilon,\tau}
, we get

\displaystyle\|\Phi_{1}(n_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau},c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau},u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau})(\cdot,t)-\Phi_{1}(n_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau},c_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau},u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau})(\cdot,t)\|_{\dot{W}^{1,2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla e^{(t-s)\Delta}\big\{\nabla\cdot\left(\frac{n_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}}{1+\tau n_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\nabla c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\chi(c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau})\right)+(u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla n_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}

\displaystyle-~\nabla\cdot\left(\frac{n_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}}{1+\tau n_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\nabla c_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\chi(c_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau})\right)-(u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla n_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\big\}(\cdot,s)\|_{L^{2}}ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}(t-s)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla\cdot\left(\frac{n_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}}{1+\tau n_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\nabla c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\chi(c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau})\right)+(u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla n_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}

\displaystyle-~\nabla\cdot\left(\frac{n_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}}{1+\tau n_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\nabla c_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\chi(c_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau})\right)-(u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla n_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}(\cdot,s)\|_{L^{2}}ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(R)T^{\frac{1}{2}}\|(n_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau},c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau},u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau})-(n_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau},c_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau},u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau})\|_{S}.

Estimates of
c^{\varepsilon,\tau}
.
For the estimate of
L^{\infty}
about
\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}
, there holds

\displaystyle\left\|\Phi_{2}(n_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau},c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau},u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau})(\cdot,t)-\Phi_{2}(n_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau},c_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau},u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau})(\cdot,t)\right\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\left\|e^{(t-s)\Delta}\left\{\frac{1}{\tau}\ln(1+\tau n_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau})\kappa(c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau})-\frac{1}{\tau}\ln(1+\tau n_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau})\kappa(c_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau})\right\}\right\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}}ds

\displaystyle+~\int_{0}^{t}\left\|e^{(t-s)\Delta}\left\{u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot\nabla c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}-u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot\nabla c_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\right\}\right\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}}ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle CT^{\frac{1}{8}}\|(n_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau},c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau},u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau})-(n_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau},c_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau},u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau})\|_{S},

where by (
2.6
), we can yield

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{\tau}\ln(1+\tau n_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau})\kappa(c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau})+u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot\nabla c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}-\frac{1}{\tau}\ln(1+\tau n_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau})\kappa(c_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau})-u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot\nabla c_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\right\|_{L^{4}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{\tau}\ln{\frac{1+\tau n_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}}{1+\tau n_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}}}\kappa(c_{2}^{\varepsilon})\right\|_{L^{4}}+\left\|\frac{1}{\tau}\ln(1+\tau n_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau})(\kappa(c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau})-\kappa(c_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}))\right\|_{L^{4}}

\displaystyle+~\left\|(u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}-u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau})\cdot\nabla c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{4}}+\|u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot\nabla(c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}-c_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau})\right\|_{L^{4}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|n_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}-n_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{4}}\|\kappa(c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau})\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|n_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\kappa^{{}^{\prime}}(\theta c_{2}^{\varepsilon}+(1-\theta)c_{1}^{\varepsilon})(c_{2}^{\varepsilon}-c_{1}^{\varepsilon})\|_{L^{4}}

\displaystyle+~\|u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}-u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{4}}\|\nabla c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla(c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}-c_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau})\|_{L^{4}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(\kappa(R),\kappa^{{}^{\prime}}(R))\|(n_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau},c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau},u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau})-(n_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau},c_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau},u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau})\|_{S}.

The estimate of
L^{2}
about
c^{\varepsilon,\tau}
is similar to the estimate of
L^{\infty}
about
\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\Phi_{2}(n_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau},c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau},u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau})(\cdot,t)-\Phi_{2}(n_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau},c_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau},u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau})(\cdot,t)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\int_{0}^{t}e^{(t-s)\Delta}\left\{\frac{1}{\tau}\ln(1+\tau n_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau})\kappa(c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau})-\frac{1}{\tau}\ln(1+\tau n_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau})\kappa(c_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau})\right\}ds\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+~\left\|\int_{0}^{t}e^{(t-s)\Delta}\left\{u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot\nabla c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}-u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot\nabla c_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\right\}ds\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\left\|e^{(t-s)\Delta}\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\ln(1+\tau n_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau})\kappa(c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau})-\frac{1}{\tau}\ln(1+\tau n_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau})\kappa(c_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau})\right)\right\|_{L^{2}}ds

\displaystyle+~\int_{0}^{t}\left\|e^{(t-s)\Delta}\left(u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot\nabla c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}-u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot\nabla c_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}}ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(R)T\|(n_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau},c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau},u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau})-(n_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau},c_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau},u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau})\|_{S}.

Moreover, we need to estimate the
\dot{W}^{2,2}
norm for
c^{\varepsilon,\tau}
. We only need to estimate the following norm

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\left\{\frac{1}{\tau}\ln(1+\tau n_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau})\kappa(c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau})+u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot\nabla c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}-\frac{1}{\tau}\ln(1+\tau n_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau})\kappa(c_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau})-u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot\nabla c_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\right\}\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\frac{\nabla n_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}}{1+\tau n_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}}-\frac{\nabla n_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}}{1+\tau n_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\right)\kappa(c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau})\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\frac{\nabla n_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}}{1+\tau n_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}}(\kappa(c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau})-\kappa(c_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}))\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+~\left\|\frac{1}{\tau}\ln{\frac{1+\tau n_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}}{1+\tau n_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}}}\kappa^{{}^{\prime}}(c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau})\nabla c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\frac{1}{\tau}\ln(1+\tau n_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau})\nabla c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}[\kappa^{{}^{\prime}}(c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau})-\kappa^{{}^{\prime}}(c_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau})]\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+~\left\|\frac{1}{\tau}\ln(1+\tau n_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau})(\nabla c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}-\nabla c_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau})\kappa^{{}^{\prime}}(c_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau})\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla(u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}-u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau})\cdot\nabla c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+~\left\|\nabla u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot\nabla(c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}-c_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau})\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|(u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}-u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau})\cdot\nabla^{2}c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot\nabla^{2}(c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}-c_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau})\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\nabla n_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}}{1+\tau n_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}}-\frac{\nabla n_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}}{1+\tau n_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\right\|_{L^{2}}\|\kappa(c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau})\|_{L^{\infty}}+\left\|\frac{\nabla n_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}}{1+\tau n_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\kappa^{{}^{\prime}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}-c_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+~\|\frac{1}{\tau}\ln{\frac{1+\tau n_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}}{1+\tau n_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}}}\|_{L^{2}}\|\kappa^{{}^{\prime}}(c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau})\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle+~\|\frac{1}{\tau}\ln(1+\tau n_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau})\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\kappa^{{}^{\prime\prime}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}-c_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+~\|\frac{1}{\tau}\ln(1+\tau n_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau})\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}-\nabla c_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}\|\kappa^{{}^{\prime}}(c_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau})\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle+~\|\nabla(u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}-u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau})\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\nabla u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla(c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}-c_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau})\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle+~\|u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}-u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla^{2}c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}+\|u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla^{2}(c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau}-c_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau})\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(\kappa(R),\kappa^{{}^{\prime}}(R),\kappa^{{}^{\prime\prime}}(R))\|(n_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau},c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau},u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau})-(n_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau},c_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau},u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau})\|_{S}.

The estimate about
u^{\varepsilon,\tau}
is similar, so we omit it.
Ultimately, one can obtain

\displaystyle\|\Phi(n_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau},c_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau},u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau})(\cdot,t)-\Phi(n_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau},c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau},u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau})(\cdot,t)\|_{S}

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle C(T^{\frac{1}{8}}+T^{\frac{1}{2}}+T)\|(n_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau},c_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau},u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\tau})-(n_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau},c_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau},u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\tau})\|_{S},

for all
t\in(0,T),
which shows that if
T
is chosen sufficiently small then
\Phi
acts as a contraction on
S.
Accordingly, Lemma
8.16
asserts
there exists
(n^{\varepsilon,\tau},c^{\varepsilon,\tau},u^{\varepsilon,\tau})\in S
, such that
\Phi(n^{\varepsilon,\tau},c^{\varepsilon,\tau},u^{\varepsilon,\tau})=(n^{\varepsilon,\tau},c^{\varepsilon,\tau},u^{\varepsilon,\tau})
.

Step III. The positivity of
n^{\varepsilon,\tau}
and
c^{\varepsilon,\tau}
in system
(
1
)
.

We know that there exists a strong solution
(n^{\varepsilon,\tau},c^{\varepsilon,\tau},u^{\varepsilon,\tau})
in
(0,T)
satisfies

\displaystyle(n^{\varepsilon,\tau},c^{\varepsilon,\tau},u^{\varepsilon,\tau})\in

\displaystyle L^{\infty}[(0,T);\{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\cap W^{1,2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\}

\displaystyle\times\{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\cap L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\cap\dot{W}^{2,2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\}\times\{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\cap\dot{W}^{2,2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\}].

Now we claim

\displaystyle n^{\varepsilon,\tau}(x,t)\geq 0\quad{\rm and}\quad c^{\varepsilon,\tau}(x,t)\geq 0\quad{\rm in}\quad\mathbb{R}^{3}\times(0,T).

(2.10)

Indeed, setting
n_{+}^{\varepsilon,\tau}=\max\{0,n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\}
,
-n_{-}^{\varepsilon,\tau}=\min\{0,n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\}
, then
n^{\varepsilon,\tau}=n_{+}^{\varepsilon,\tau}-n_{-}^{\varepsilon,\tau}
. Multiply the first equation of (
1.5
) by
n_{-}^{\varepsilon,\tau}
, we arrive

\displaystyle\partial_{t}n_{-}^{\varepsilon,\tau}n_{-}^{\varepsilon,\tau}+(u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla n_{-}^{\varepsilon,\tau}n_{-}^{\varepsilon,\tau}-\Delta n_{-}^{\varepsilon,\tau}n_{-}^{\varepsilon,\tau}=-\nabla\cdot\left(\frac{n_{+}^{\varepsilon,\tau}}{1+\tau n_{+}^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\chi(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})\right)n_{-}^{\varepsilon,\tau}.

Then integrating by parts on the above equation, we obtain

\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\|n_{-}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla n_{-}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=0,

which follows

\displaystyle\|n_{-}^{\varepsilon,\tau}(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+2\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla n_{-}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=\|n_{-}^{\varepsilon,\tau}(\cdot,0)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=\|n_{-}^{\varepsilon}(\cdot,0)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Note that
n_{-}^{\varepsilon}(\cdot,0)=0
due to
n_{0}^{\varepsilon}=n_{0}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon}\geq 0,
there holds

\displaystyle\|n_{-}^{\varepsilon,\tau}(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+2\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla n_{-}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=0,

which mean
n_{-}^{\varepsilon,\tau}=0
. Thus
n^{\varepsilon,\tau}=n_{+}^{\varepsilon,\tau},
we have
n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\geq 0.

For the part of
c^{\varepsilon,\tau}
, setting
c_{+}^{\varepsilon,\tau}=\max\{0,c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\}
,
-c_{-}^{\varepsilon,\tau}=\min\{0,c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\}
, then
c^{\varepsilon,\tau}=c_{+}^{\varepsilon,\tau}-c_{-}^{\varepsilon,\tau}.
For the second of (
1
), we multiply it by
c_{-}^{\varepsilon,\tau}
and obtain

\displaystyle\partial_{t}c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot c_{-}^{\varepsilon,\tau}+u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot c_{-}^{\varepsilon,\tau}-\Delta c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot c_{-}^{\varepsilon,\tau}+\frac{1}{\tau}\ln(1+\tau n^{\varepsilon,\tau})\kappa(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})\cdot c_{-}^{\varepsilon,\tau}=0.

Integrate by parts and note that
\kappa(0)=0
,
\kappa^{\prime}(s)\geq 0
and
\theta\in(0,1)
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\|c_{-}^{\varepsilon,\tau}(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla c_{-}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{1}{\tau}\ln(1+\tau n^{\varepsilon,\tau})\kappa(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})\cdot c_{-}^{\varepsilon,\tau}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{\kappa(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})-\kappa(0)}{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\cdot c_{-}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ln(1+\tau n^{\varepsilon,\tau})c_{-}^{\varepsilon,\tau}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{\kappa^{{}^{\prime}}(\theta c^{\varepsilon,\tau})c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}c_{-}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ln(1+\tau n^{\varepsilon,\tau})c_{-}^{\varepsilon,\tau}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 0,

which means
c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\geq 0
. We prove the claim
(
2.10
)
.

Step IV. The mass.

We claim that

\displaystyle||n^{\varepsilon,\tau}(x,t)||_{L^{1}}=||n_{0}^{\varepsilon}(x)||_{L^{1}},

(2.11)

and

\displaystyle||c^{\varepsilon,\tau}(x,t)||_{L^{1}\cap L^{\infty}}\leq||c_{0}^{\varepsilon}(x)||_{L^{1}\cap L^{\infty}},

(2.12)

for any
(x,t)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\times(0,T).

Proof.

Recall the first equation of
(
1
)
as follow:

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{t}n^{\varepsilon,\tau}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla n^{\varepsilon,\tau}-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Delta n^{\varepsilon,\tau}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\nabla\cdot\left(\frac{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}}{1+\tau n^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\chi(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})\right)=0.

Integration by parts and divergence theorem yield that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}n^{\varepsilon,\tau}=0,

which means for any
t\in(0,T)
,

\displaystyle\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}(x,t)\|_{L^{1}}-\|n^{\varepsilon}(x,0)\|_{L^{1}}=0.

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\|c^{\varepsilon,\tau}(x,t)\|_{L^{1}}-\|c^{\varepsilon}(x,0)\|_{L^{1}}\leq 0.

(2.13)

For the
L^{\infty}
norm of
c^{\varepsilon,\tau}
, since
\kappa(s)>0
, by the maximum principle, we know that

\displaystyle\|c^{\varepsilon,\tau}(x,t)\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq\|c_{0}^{\varepsilon}(x)\|_{L^{\infty}}.

The proof of (
2.11
) and (
2.12
) is complete.

Step V. Higher regularity and global existence.

Let
E^{\varepsilon,\tau}=(n^{\varepsilon,\tau},c^{\varepsilon,\tau},\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau},u^{\varepsilon,\tau})
,
E_{0}^{\varepsilon}=(n_{0}^{\varepsilon},c_{0}^{\varepsilon},\nabla c_{0}^{\varepsilon},u_{0}^{\varepsilon})
. Then the solution is global
if
the following uniform estimates hold

\displaystyle\|E^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{2}_{x}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\|E^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{H^{3}}^{2}

(2.14)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(\varepsilon,\tau,\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^{\infty}},\|\chi\|_{0},\|\kappa\|_{0},\|n_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{1}},\|E_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{2}})(T+1)^{54},

Next, we will estimate the above norms separately.

i. The energy norms of
c^{\varepsilon,\tau}:

\displaystyle\|c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq\|c_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}^{2},~~{\rm{for}}~~{\rm{any}}~t\in(0,T).

(2.15)

For the second equation of
(
1
)
, multiplying it by
c^{\varepsilon,\tau}
and integrating with respect to space variable
x
over
\mathbb{R}^{3}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\|c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}c^{\varepsilon,\tau}-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{\ln(1+\tau n^{\varepsilon,\tau})}{\tau}\kappa(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})c^{\varepsilon,\tau}.

Since
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}c^{\varepsilon,\tau}=0
,
\kappa(s)\geq 0
, by
(
2.10
)
we have

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\|c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq 0,

which means (
2.15
) holds.

ii. The energy norms of
n^{\varepsilon,\tau}:

\displaystyle\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

(2.16)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left(\tau,\|\chi\|_{0},\|n_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}},\|c_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}\right),~~{\rm{for}}~~{\rm{any}}~t\in(0,T).

For the first equation of
(
1
)
, multiplying it by
n^{\varepsilon,\tau}
and integrating with respect to space variable
x
over
\mathbb{R}^{3}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}}{1+\tau n^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\chi(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})\cdot\nabla n^{\varepsilon,\tau}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\|\nabla n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C(\tau)\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|\chi(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2},

where we use
\frac{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}}{1+\tau n^{\varepsilon,\tau}}<\tau^{-1}.

Noting that
\chi\in C^{1}(\overline{\mathbb{R}^{+}})
, we know that
\|\chi(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq\|\chi\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\|c_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}})}
, which yields

\frac{d}{dt}\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq C\left(\tau,\|\chi\|_{0}\right)\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Then (
2.16
) holds by (
2.15
).

iii. The energy norms of
u^{\varepsilon,\tau}:

\displaystyle\|u^{\varepsilon,\tau}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

(2.17)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left(\tau,\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^{\infty}},\|\chi\|_{0},\|n_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{1}},\|E_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}\right)(T+1),~~{\rm{for}}~~{\rm{any}}~t\in(0,T).

Multiplying the third equation of
(
1
)
by
u^{\varepsilon,\tau}
and integrating with respect to space variable
x
over
\mathbb{R}^{3}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\|u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\nabla\phi)\ast\rho^{\varepsilon}\cdot u^{\varepsilon,\tau}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~C\left(\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}\right)\|u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{6}}\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\frac{6}{5}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\frac{1}{2}\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\left(\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}\right)\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\frac{6}{5}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\frac{1}{2}\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\left(\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}\right)\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{1}}^{\frac{4}{3}}\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{2}{3}}

Integrating with respect to
t
, by (
2.16
) we get (
2.17
).

iv. the energy norms of
\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\tau}:

\displaystyle\|u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{1}_{x}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}

(2.18)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left(\varepsilon,\tau,\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^{\infty}},\|\chi\|_{0},\|n_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{1}},\|E_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{1}}\right)(T+1)^{2},~~{\rm{for}}~~{\rm{any}}~t\in(0,T).

Rewrite the third equation of (
1
) as follow:

\partial_{t}\partial_{i}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}+\partial_{i}\left(\mu(u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\right)-\partial_{i}\Delta u^{\varepsilon,\tau}+\partial_{i}\nabla P^{\varepsilon,\tau}=-\partial_{i}\left((n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\nabla\phi)\ast\rho^{\varepsilon}\right),

with
i=1,2,3.
Multiplying it by
\partial_{i}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}
and integrating with respect to space variable
x
over
\mathbb{R}^{3}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla^{2}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{i}\left(\mu(u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\right)\cdot\partial_{i}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}

\displaystyle-~\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{i}\left((n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\nabla\phi)\ast\rho^{\varepsilon}\right)\cdot\partial_{i}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle A_{1}+A_{2}.

Integration by parts, Hölder’s inequality and Young’s inequality yield that

\displaystyle A_{1}=

\displaystyle\mu\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left((u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\right)\Delta u^{\varepsilon,\tau}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}\|\Delta u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{16}\|\nabla^{2}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C(\varepsilon)\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Similarly, for
A_{2},
by integration by parts, Hölder’s inequality and Young’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle A_{2}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\|\nabla^{2}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\left(\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}\right)\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Combining
A_{1}
and
A_{2},
there holds

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla^{2}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(\varepsilon)\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\left(\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}\right)\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

By (
2.16
) and (
2.17
), we obtain (
2.18
).

v. the energy norms of
\nabla^{2}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}:

\displaystyle\|u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{2}_{x}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{H^{2}}^{2}

(2.19)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left(\varepsilon,\tau,\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^{\infty}},\|\chi\|_{0},\|n_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{1}},\|E_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{2}}\right)(T+1)^{3},~~{\rm{for}}~~{\rm{any}}~t\in(0,T).

We write the third equation of
(
1
)
as follow:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\partial_{ij}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}+\mu\partial_{ij}((u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\tau})+\partial_{ij}\nabla P^{\varepsilon,\tau}=\partial_{ij}\Delta u^{\varepsilon,\tau}-\partial_{ij}((n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\nabla\phi)\ast\rho^{\varepsilon}).

Multiplying above equation by
\partial_{ij}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}
, integrating with respect to space variable
x
over
\mathbb{R}^{3}
and noting that
\nabla\cdot u^{\varepsilon,\tau}=0
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\|\nabla^{2}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla^{3}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\mu\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{ij}((u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\tau})\cdot\partial_{ij}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{ij}((n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\nabla\phi)\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot\partial_{ij}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle B_{1}+B_{2}.

By the product derivative rule, we have

\displaystyle B_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\mu\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{ij}(u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot\partial_{ij}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}dx-\mu\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla\partial_{ij}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot\partial_{ij}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}dx

\displaystyle-\mu\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{j}(u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla\partial_{i}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot\partial_{ij}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}dx-\mu\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{i}(u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla\partial_{j}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot\partial_{ij}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}dx

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle B_{11}+B_{12}+B_{13}+B_{14}.

For
B_{11}
, noting that

\displaystyle\|\partial_{ij}(u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\|_{L^{\infty}}=\|(\partial_{ij}u^{\varepsilon,\tau})\ast\rho^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\|\nabla^{2}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}\|\rho^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}\leq C(\varepsilon)\|\nabla^{2}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}},

integration by parts yields that

\displaystyle B_{11}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mu\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\partial_{ij}(u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla\partial_{ij}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{16}\|\nabla^{3}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\|u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|\partial_{ij}(u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{16}\|\nabla^{3}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C(\varepsilon)\|u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|\nabla^{2}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

For
B_{12}
, obviously

B_{12}=0.

For
B_{13}
and
B_{14}
, specially, we calculate
B_{13}
in detail.

\displaystyle B_{13}

\displaystyle=\mu

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{i}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\partial_{j}(u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla\partial_{ij}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{16}\|\nabla^{3}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C(\varepsilon)\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Collecting
B_{11}
to
B_{14}
, we have

\displaystyle B_{1}\leq\frac{3}{16}\|\nabla^{3}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C(\varepsilon)\|u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|\nabla^{2}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C(\varepsilon)\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

For the term
B_{2}
, using Hölder’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle B_{2}

\displaystyle\leq~\|\nabla^{2}((n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\nabla\phi)\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla^{2}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq~C(\varepsilon)\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\nabla\phi\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla^{2}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq~C(\varepsilon)\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\|\nabla^{2}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Collecting
B_{1}
and
B_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\|\nabla^{2}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla^{3}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(\varepsilon)\left(\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}+\|u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)\|\nabla^{2}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C(\varepsilon)\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

By (
2.16
) and (
2.18
), we obtain (
2.19
).

vi. the energy norms of
\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}:

\displaystyle\|c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{1}_{x}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}

(2.20)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left(\tau,\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^{\infty}},\|\chi\|_{0},\|\kappa\|_{0},\|n_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{1}},\|E_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{1}}\right)(T+1)^{\frac{5}{2}},~~{\rm{for}}~~{\rm{any}}~t\in(0,T).

Rewrite the second equation of (
1
) as follow:

\partial_{t}\partial_{i}c^{\varepsilon,\tau}+\partial_{i}\left(u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\right)-\partial_{i}\Delta c^{\varepsilon,\tau}=-\partial_{i}\left(\frac{\ln(1+\tau n^{\varepsilon,\tau})}{\tau}\kappa(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})\right)

with
i=1,2,3.
Multiplying it by
\partial_{i}c^{\varepsilon,\tau}
and integrating with respect to space variable
x
over
\mathbb{R}^{3}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla^{2}c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{i}\left(u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\right)\partial_{i}c^{\varepsilon,\tau}

\displaystyle-~\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{i}\left(\frac{\ln(1+\tau n^{\varepsilon,\tau})}{\tau}\kappa(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})\right)\partial_{i}c^{\varepsilon,\tau}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle C_{1}+C_{2}.

The term
C_{1}
is similar as
A_{1},
we omit the proof and arrive

\displaystyle C_{1}\leq~\frac{1}{16}\|\nabla^{2}c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}.

By the product derivative rule for
C_{2},
we have

\displaystyle C_{2}=

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{\partial_{i}n^{\varepsilon,\tau}}{1+\tau n^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\kappa(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})\partial_{i}c^{\varepsilon,\tau}-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{\ln(1+\tau n^{\varepsilon,\tau})}{\tau}\kappa^{\prime}(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})\partial_{i}c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\partial_{i}c^{\varepsilon,\tau}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle C_{21}+C_{22}.

For
C_{21},
using Hölder’s inequality and Young’s inequality, we get

\displaystyle C_{21}\leq~\|\nabla n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}\|\kappa(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}

For
C_{22},
note that

\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{4}}\leq C\|c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{8}}\|\nabla^{2}c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{7}{8}},

then

\displaystyle C_{22}\leq

\displaystyle\|\frac{\ln(1+\tau n^{\varepsilon,\tau})}{\tau}\|_{L^{2}}\|\kappa^{\prime}(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})\|_{L^{\infty}}\||\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{2}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(\|\kappa\|_{0})\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}\|c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{4}}\|\nabla^{2}c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{7}{4}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{16}\|\nabla^{2}c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\left(\|\kappa\|_{0}\right)\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{8}\|c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Collecting
C_{21}
and
C_{22}
yields that

\displaystyle C_{2}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{16}\|\nabla^{2}c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}\|\kappa(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+~C\left(\|\kappa\|_{0}\right)\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{8}\|c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Then combining
C_{1}
and
C_{2},
we have

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla^{2}c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\nabla n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}\|\kappa(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}+C\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}

\displaystyle+C\left(\|\kappa\|_{0}\right)\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{8}\|c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Note that
\|u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\|u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{4}}\|\nabla^{2}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{3}{4}},
by (
2.16
), (
2.17
),(
2.19
) and (
2.15
), we obtain (
2.20
).

vii. the energy norms of
|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{\frac{3}{2}}:

\displaystyle\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{3}_{x}}^{3}+\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla(|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{\frac{3}{2}})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

(2.21)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left(\tau,\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^{\infty}},\|\chi\|_{0},\|\kappa\|_{0},\|n_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{1}},\|E_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{1}}\right)(T+1)^{\frac{21}{4}},~~{\rm{for}}~~{\rm{any}}~t\in(0,T).

Rewrite the second equation of (
1
) as follow:

\partial_{t}\partial_{i}c^{\varepsilon,\tau}+\partial_{i}\left(u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\right)-\partial_{i}\Delta c^{\varepsilon,\tau}=-\partial_{i}\left(\frac{\ln(1+\tau n^{\varepsilon,\tau})}{\tau}\kappa(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})\right)

with
i=1,2,3.
Multiplying it by
|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}|\partial_{i}c^{\varepsilon,\tau}
and integrating with respect to space variable
x
over
\mathbb{R}^{3}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3}\frac{d}{dt}\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{3}}^{3}+\frac{8}{9}\|\nabla(|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{\frac{3}{2}})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{i}\left(u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\right)|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}|\partial_{i}c^{\varepsilon,\tau}dx\right|

\displaystyle+~\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{i}\left(\frac{\ln(1+\tau n^{\varepsilon,\tau})}{\tau}\kappa(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})\right)|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}|\partial_{i}c^{\varepsilon,\tau}dx\right|

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle|D_{1}|+|D_{2}|.

For
D_{1},
we have

\displaystyle D_{1}\leq~C\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{6}}\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\frac{18}{5}}}^{3}\leq~C\|\nabla^{2}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\frac{18}{5}}}^{3}.

By the product derivative rule for
D_{2},
we obtain

\displaystyle D_{2}

\displaystyle=~\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{i}\left(\frac{\ln(1+\tau n^{\varepsilon,\tau})}{\tau}\kappa(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})\right)|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}|\partial_{i}c^{\varepsilon,\tau}

\displaystyle=~\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{\partial_{i}n^{\varepsilon,\tau}}{1+\tau n^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\kappa(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}|\partial_{i}c^{\varepsilon,\tau}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{\ln(1+\tau n^{\varepsilon,\tau})}{\tau}\kappa^{\prime}(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})\partial_{i}c^{\varepsilon,\tau}|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}|\partial_{i}c^{\varepsilon,\tau}

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle=~D_{21}+D_{22}.

For
D_{21},
by interpolation inequality
\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|^{\frac{3}{2}}_{L^{4}}\leq C\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|^{\frac{15}{16}}_{L^{3}}\|\nabla(|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{\frac{3}{2}})\|^{\frac{3}{8}}_{L^{2}}
,Hölder’s inequality and Young’s inequality, we get

\displaystyle D_{21}

\displaystyle\leq~C\left(\|\kappa\|_{0}\right)\|\nabla n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{4}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq~C\left(\|\kappa\|_{0}\right)\|\nabla n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|^{\frac{5}{4}}_{L^{3}}\|\nabla(|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{\frac{3}{2}})\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq~\frac{1}{16}\|\nabla(|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{\frac{3}{2}})\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+C\left(\|\kappa\|_{0}\right)\|\nabla n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{4}{3}}\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|^{\frac{5}{3}}_{L^{3}}

For
D_{22},
similar to
D_{21}
, by interpolation inequality
\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{6}}\leq C\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{3}}^{\frac{1}{4}}\|\nabla(|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{\frac{3}{2}})\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}
, we obtain

\displaystyle D_{22}

\displaystyle\leq~C\left(\|\kappa\|_{0}\right)\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{6}}^{3}

\displaystyle\leq~C\left(\|\kappa\|_{0}\right)\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{3}}^{\frac{3}{4}}\|\nabla(|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{\frac{3}{2}})\|^{\frac{3}{2}}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq~\frac{1}{16}\|\nabla(|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{\frac{3}{2}})\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+C\left(\|\kappa\|_{0}\right)\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|^{4}_{L^{2}}\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{3}}^{3}.

Collecting
D_{21}
and
D_{22},
we have

\displaystyle D_{2}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{8}\|\nabla(|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{\frac{3}{2}})\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+C\left(\|\kappa\|_{0}\right)\|\nabla n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{4}{3}}\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|^{\frac{5}{3}}_{L^{3}}

\displaystyle+C\left(\|\kappa\|_{0}\right)\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|^{4}_{L^{2}}\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{3}}^{3}.

Combining with
D_{1}
and
D_{2},
there holds

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{3}}^{3}+\|\nabla(|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{\frac{3}{2}})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|\nabla^{2}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\frac{18}{5}}}^{3}+C\left(\|\kappa\|_{0}\right)\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|^{4}_{L^{2}}\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|^{\frac{3}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|\nabla^{2}c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|^{\frac{3}{2}}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+C\left(\|\kappa\|_{0}\right)\|\nabla n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{4}{3}}\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|^{\frac{5}{3}}_{L^{3}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|\nabla^{2}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\frac{18}{5}}}^{3}+C\left(\|\kappa\|_{0}\right)\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|^{4}_{L^{2}}\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|^{\frac{3}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|\nabla^{2}c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|^{\frac{3}{2}}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+C\left(\|\kappa\|_{0}\right)\|\nabla n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{4}{3}}\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|^{\frac{20}{21}}_{L^{2}}\|\nabla(|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{\frac{3}{2}})\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{10}{21}}.

By (
2.15
), (
2.16
), (
2.20
) and (
2.17
), we have (
2.21
).

viii. the energy norms of
|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{\frac{5}{2}}:

\displaystyle\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{5}_{x}}^{5}+\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla(|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{\frac{5}{2}})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

(2.22)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left(\tau,\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^{\infty}},\|\chi\|_{0},\|\kappa\|_{0},\|n_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{1}},\|E_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{2}}\right)(T+1)^{\frac{41}{4}},~~{\rm{for}}~~{\rm{any}}~t\in(0,T).

Rewrite the second equation of (
1
) as follow:

\partial_{t}\partial_{i}c^{\varepsilon,\tau}+\partial_{i}\left(u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\right)-\partial_{i}\Delta c^{\varepsilon,\tau}=-\partial_{i}\left(\frac{\ln(1+\tau n^{\varepsilon,\tau})}{\tau}\kappa(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})\right)

with
i=1,2,3.
Multiplying it by
|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{3}\partial_{i}c^{\varepsilon,\tau}
and integrating with respect to space variable
x
over
\mathbb{R}^{3}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{5}\frac{d}{dt}\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{5}}^{5}+\frac{16}{25}\|\nabla(|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{\frac{5}{2}})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{i}\left(u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\right)|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{3}\partial_{i}c^{\varepsilon,\tau}dx\right|

\displaystyle+~\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{i}\left(\frac{\ln(1+\tau n^{\varepsilon,\tau})}{\tau}\kappa(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})\right)|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{3}\partial_{i}c^{\varepsilon,\tau}dx\right|

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle|D_{1}^{\prime}|+|D_{2}^{\prime}|.

For
D_{1}^{\prime},
note that
\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{6}}^{5}\leq C\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{5}}^{\frac{15}{4}}\|\nabla|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{\frac{5}{2}}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}},

we have

\displaystyle D_{1}^{\prime}\leq~C\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{6}}\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{6}}^{5}\leq~C\|\nabla^{2}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{5}}^{\frac{15}{4}}\|\nabla|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{\frac{5}{2}}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}.

By the product derivative rule for
D_{2}^{\prime},
we obtain

\displaystyle D_{2}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=~\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{i}\left(\frac{\ln(1+\tau n^{\varepsilon,\tau})}{\tau}\kappa(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})\right)|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{3}\partial_{i}c^{\varepsilon,\tau}

\displaystyle=~\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{\partial_{i}n^{\varepsilon,\tau}}{1+\tau n^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\kappa(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{3}\partial_{i}c^{\varepsilon,\tau}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{\ln(1+\tau n^{\varepsilon,\tau})}{\tau}\kappa^{\prime}(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})\partial_{i}c^{\varepsilon,\tau}|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{3}\partial_{i}c^{\varepsilon,\tau}

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle=~D_{21}^{\prime}+D_{22}^{\prime}.

For
D_{21}^{\prime},
we get

\displaystyle D_{21}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq~C\left(\|\kappa\|_{0}\right)\|\nabla n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{8}}^{4}

\displaystyle\leq~C\left(\|\kappa\|_{0}\right)\|\nabla n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|^{\frac{7}{4}}_{L^{5}}\|\nabla(|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{\frac{5}{2}})\|^{\frac{9}{10}}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq~\frac{1}{16}\|\nabla(|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{\frac{5}{2}})\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+C\left(\|\kappa\|_{0}\right)\|\nabla n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{20}{11}}\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|^{\frac{35}{11}}_{L^{5}}

For
D_{22}^{\prime}
, we obtain

\displaystyle D_{22}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq~C\left(\|\kappa\|_{0}\right)\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{10}}^{5}

\displaystyle\leq~C\left(\|\kappa\|_{0}\right)\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{5}}^{\frac{5}{4}}\|\nabla(|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{\frac{5}{2}})\|^{\frac{3}{2}}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq~\frac{1}{16}\|\nabla(|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{\frac{5}{2}})\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+C\left(\|\kappa\|_{0}\right)\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|^{4}_{L^{2}}\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{5}}^{5}.

Collecting
D_{21}^{\prime}
and
D_{22}^{\prime},
we have

\displaystyle D_{2}^{\prime}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{8}\|\nabla(|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{\frac{5}{2}})\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+C\left(\|\kappa\|_{0}\right)\|\nabla n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{20}{11}}\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|^{\frac{35}{11}}_{L^{5}}
