\displaystyle+~\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}\nabla\left(\frac{\kappa(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})}{2\tau\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}}\right)~~\ln(1+\tau n^{\varepsilon,\tau})\cdot\nabla\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\psi

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{6}\sum_{i=j}\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}(\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}})^{-2}(\partial_{j}\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}})^{2}(\partial_{i}\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}})^{2}\psi

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|\nabla\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}|^{2}(\partial_{t}\psi+\Delta\psi)+\frac{1}{2}\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|\nabla\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}|^{2}(u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla\psi

\displaystyle+~\frac{9}{2}\|{c^{\varepsilon}_{0}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{2}\psi-\frac{1}{4}\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}\frac{\kappa(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})}{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\frac{1}{1+\tau n^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\nabla n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\psi.

Since
\kappa(s)=\Theta_{0}s\chi(s),
we have

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\Theta_{0}}\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}\frac{\kappa(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})}{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\frac{\nabla n^{\varepsilon,\tau}}{1+\tau n^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\cdot\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\psi=-\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}\frac{\chi(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})}{1+\tau n^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\nabla n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\psi.

(4.9)

Recall the local estimate of
n^{\varepsilon,\tau}
in (
4.2
), by taking

(\ref{n})+\frac{4}{\Theta_{0}}\times(\ref{sqrt c varepsilon tau})
and using by (
4.9
) and (
3.13
), we arrive at,

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}(n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ln(n^{\varepsilon,\tau})\psi)(\cdot,t)+4\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|\nabla\sqrt{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}}|^{2}\psi

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{\Theta_{0}}\int_{\Omega}(|\nabla\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}|^{2}\psi)(\cdot,t)+\frac{4}{3\Theta_{0}}\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|\Delta\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}|^{2}\psi

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{3\Theta_{0}}\sum_{i=j}\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}(\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}})^{-2}(\partial_{j}\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}})^{2}(\partial_{i}\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}})^{2}\psi

\displaystyle\leq\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ln n^{\varepsilon,\tau}(\partial_{t}\psi+\Delta\psi)+\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ln n^{\varepsilon,\tau}(u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla\psi

\displaystyle+\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}\left(\frac{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}}{1+\tau n^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\chi(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})\right)\ln n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot\nabla\psi+\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}\left(\frac{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}}{1+\tau n^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\chi(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})\right)\cdot\nabla\psi

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{\Theta_{0}}\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|\nabla\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}|^{2}(\partial_{t}\psi+\Delta\psi)+\frac{2}{\Theta_{0}}\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|\nabla\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}|^{2}{(u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})}\cdot\nabla\psi

\displaystyle+\frac{18}{\Theta_{0}}\|{c^{\varepsilon}_{0}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{2}\psi.

(4.10)

For the equation of
u^{\varepsilon,\tau}
, multiplying
(\ref{eq:CNS})_{3}
by
2u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\psi
and integration by parts, we have

\displaystyle\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}\partial_{t}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot 2u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\psi+\mu\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}(u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot 2u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\psi-\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}\Delta u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot 2u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\psi

\displaystyle+\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}\nabla P^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot 2u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\psi+\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}(n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\nabla\phi)\ast\rho^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot 2u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\psi=0.

Integration by parts achieves that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}(|u^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{2})(\cdot,t)\psi+2\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{2}\psi

(4.11)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|u^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{2}\left(\partial_{t}\psi+\Delta\psi\right)+\mu\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|u^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{2}(u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla\psi

\displaystyle+2\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}(P^{\varepsilon,\tau}-\bar{P}^{\varepsilon,\tau})u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot\nabla\psi-2\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}(n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\nabla\phi)\ast\rho^{\varepsilon}\cdot u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\psi.

Then
(\ref{ine:energy u})\times\frac{18}{\Theta_{0}}\|{c^{\varepsilon}_{0}}\|_{L^{\infty}}+(\ref{ine:energy n1 c1})
yields (
4.8
). The proof is complete.

Remark 4.9
.

It follows from (
4.8
) that

\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|\nabla\sqrt{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}}|^{2}\leq C.

(4.12)

Indeed, for the bounded domain
\Omega
,
using
|\ln n|n^{\alpha}\leq\alpha^{-1}e^{-1}
for
0<n<1
and
0<\alpha<\frac{1}{20}
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}(n^{\varepsilon,\tau}|\ln n^{\varepsilon,\tau}|)(\cdot,t)dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}(n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ln n^{\varepsilon,\tau})(\cdot,t)dx-2\int_{\Omega\cap\{x;0<n^{\varepsilon,\tau}<1\}}(n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ln n^{\varepsilon,\tau})(\cdot,t)dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}(n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ln n^{\varepsilon,\tau})(\cdot,t)dx+2\alpha^{-1}e^{-1}\int_{\Omega}(n^{\varepsilon,\tau(1-\alpha)})(\cdot,t)dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}(n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ln n^{\varepsilon,\tau})(\cdot,t)dx+2\alpha^{-1}e^{-1}C^{1-\alpha}.

thus adding
2\alpha^{-1}e^{-1}C^{1-\alpha}
to both sides of the inequality of (
4.8
), the proof is complete.

5.
The convergence of
u^{\varepsilon,\tau}
,
n^{\varepsilon,\tau}
and
c^{\varepsilon,\tau}

In the following, we prove the strong convergence of
u^{\varepsilon,\tau}
,
n^{\varepsilon,\tau}
and
c^{\varepsilon,\tau}
respectively by Aubin-Lions Lemma.

Lemma 5.10
.

u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\rightarrow u^{\varepsilon}
in
L^{q}(0,t;L^{q}(\Omega)
) strongly for
q\in[2,\frac{10}{3})
as
\tau\to 0
.

Proof.
Recalling the equation of
u^{\varepsilon,\tau}
of
(\ref{eq:GKS})_{3}
,
for any smooth function
\zeta\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega\times(0,t))
with
\nabla\cdot\zeta=0
, there holds

\displaystyle\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}\partial_{t}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot\zeta

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}(0,t;L^{2}(\Omega))}\|\nabla\zeta\|_{L^{2}(0,t;L^{2}(\Omega))}

\displaystyle+~C\|u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\frac{8}{3}}(0,t;L^{4}(\Omega))}\|u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\frac{8}{3}}(0,t;L^{4}(\Omega))}\|\nabla\zeta\|_{L^{4}(0,t;L^{2}(\Omega))}

\displaystyle+~C\|(n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\nabla\phi)\ast\rho^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\frac{5}{3}}(0,t;L^{\frac{6}{5}}(\Omega))}\|\zeta\|_{L^{\frac{5}{2}}(0,t;L^{6}(\Omega))}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}(0,t;L^{2}(\Omega))}\|\nabla\zeta\|_{L^{2}(0,t;L^{2}(\Omega))}+C\|u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\frac{8}{3}}(0,t;L^{4}(\Omega))}^{2}\|\nabla\zeta\|_{L^{4}(0,t;L^{2}(\Omega))}

\displaystyle+~C\|\nabla\phi\|_{{L^{\infty}}(\Omega)}\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\frac{5}{3}}(0,t;L^{\frac{6}{5}}(\Omega))}\|\nabla\zeta\|_{L^{\frac{5}{2}}(0,t;L^{2}(\Omega))}.

By
(\ref{ine: energy})
and (
4.12
), we know
n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\in L^{\infty}(0,t;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))
and
\nabla\sqrt{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\in L^{2}(0,t;L^{2}(\Omega))
, note that

\displaystyle\|\sqrt{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\|_{L^{q}_{t}L^{p}_{x}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(\|\sqrt{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}+\|\nabla\sqrt{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}),\quad\frac{3}{p}+\frac{2}{q}=\frac{3}{2},\quad 2\leq p\leq 6,

especially, we have

\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\frac{5}{3}}(0,t;L^{\frac{5}{3}}(\Omega))}\leq C.

(5.1)

Since
\Omega
is bounded, it follows that

\displaystyle\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\frac{5}{3}}(0,t;L^{\frac{6}{5}}(\Omega))}\leq C(|\Omega|)\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\frac{5}{3}}(0,t;L^{\frac{5}{3}}(\Omega))}\leq C.

Similarly, we have

\|u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\frac{8}{3}}(0,t;L^{4}(\Omega))}\leq C.

(5.2)

Due to
u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\in L^{\infty}(0,t;L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))
and
\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\in L^{2}(0,t;L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))
from (
3.5
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}\partial_{t}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot\zeta

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left(\|\nabla\zeta\|_{L^{\frac{5}{2}}(0,t;L^{2}(\Omega))}+\|\nabla\zeta\|_{L^{2}(0,t;L^{2}(\Omega))}+\|\nabla\zeta\|_{L^{4}(0,t;L^{2}(\Omega))}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(t,|\Omega|)\|\nabla\zeta\|_{L^{4}(0,t;L^{2}(\Omega))},

which means that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\in L^{\frac{4}{3}}(0,t;(\dot{W}^{1,2}_{\sigma}(\Omega))^{\prime}).

Noting that
u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\in L^{2}(0,t;\dot{W}^{1,2}_{\sigma}(\Omega))
, by Lemma
8.18
, we have

\displaystyle u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\rightarrow u^{\varepsilon}\quad{\rm in}\quad L^{2}(0,t;L^{2}_{\sigma}(\Omega))

as
\tau\to 0.
Furthermore, we can obtain

\displaystyle u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\rightarrow u^{\varepsilon}\quad{\rm in}\quad L^{q}(0,t;L^{q}(\Omega))\quad{\rm for}\quad q\in[2,\frac{10}{3})

as
\tau\to 0.

Lemma 5.11
.

\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\rightarrow\nabla c^{\varepsilon}
in
L^{q}(0,t;L^{q}(\Omega)
) strongly for
q\in[2,\frac{10}{3})
as
\tau\to 0
.

Proof.
Recall the equation of
c^{\varepsilon,\tau}
of
(\ref{eq:GKS})_{2}
.
Taking the derivative of the above equation, we have

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}+\nabla(u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau})-\Delta\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}=-\nabla\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\ln(1+\tau n^{\varepsilon,\tau})\kappa(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})\right).

For any smooth function
\zeta\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega\times(0,t))
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}\partial_{t}\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot\zeta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\nabla\cdot\zeta-\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}\Delta c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\nabla\cdot\zeta

\displaystyle+~\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\ln(1+\tau n^{\varepsilon,\tau})\kappa(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})\right)\nabla\cdot\zeta

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\frac{8}{3}}(0,t;L^{4}(\Omega))}\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\frac{8}{3}}(0,t;L^{4}(\Omega))}\|\nabla\zeta\|_{L^{4}(0,t;L^{2}(\Omega))}

\displaystyle+~C\|\Delta c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}(0,t;L^{2}(\Omega))}\|\nabla\zeta\|_{L^{2}(0,t;L^{2}(\Omega))}

\displaystyle+~C\|\kappa\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\|c_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}})}\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\frac{4}{3}}(0,t;L^{2}(\Omega))}\|\nabla\zeta\|_{L^{4}(0,t;L^{2}(\Omega))}.

Since
\nabla\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\in L^{\infty}(0,t;L^{2}(\Omega))\cap L^{2}(0,t;\dot{H}^{1}(\Omega)),
there holds

\|\nabla\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\|_{L^{\frac{8}{3}}(0,t;L^{4}(\Omega))}\leq C.

(5.3)

Similarly, we obtain

\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\frac{4}{3}}(0,t;L^{2}(\Omega))}\leq C.

(5.4)

Noting that

\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}=2\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\nabla\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}},

and
\|c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,t;L^{\infty}(\Omega))}\leq\|c_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C,

we have

\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\frac{8}{3}}(0,t;L^{4}(\Omega))}\leq 2\|c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,t;L^{\infty}(\Omega))}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\|_{L^{\frac{8}{3}}(0,t;L^{4}(\Omega))}\leq C.

(5.5)

Noting that

\displaystyle\Delta c^{\varepsilon,\tau}=2\nabla\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\otimes\nabla\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}+2\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\Delta\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}},

we arrive

\displaystyle\|\Delta c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}(0,t;L^{2}(\Omega))}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|\nabla\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}|^{4}+2\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}c^{\varepsilon,\tau}|\Delta\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}|^{2}

(5.6)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\|c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,t;L^{\infty}(\Omega))}\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}(\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}})^{-2}|\nabla\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}|^{4}

\displaystyle+~2\|c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,t;L^{\infty}(\Omega))}\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|\Delta\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}|^{2}.

By (
3.5
) of Lemma
3.7
, we have
\|\Delta c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}(0,t;L^{2}(\Omega))}\leq C
,
where use
\Delta\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\in L^{2}(0,t;L^{2}(\Omega))
and
\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}(\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}})^{-2}|\nabla\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}|^{4}\leq C
. Combining (
5.2
) and (
5.4
)-(
5.6
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot\zeta

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left(\|\nabla\zeta\|_{L^{2}(0,t;L^{2}(\Omega))}+\|\nabla\zeta\|_{L^{4}(0,t;L^{2}(\Omega))}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(t)\|\nabla\zeta\|_{L^{4}(0,t;L^{2}(\Omega))},

which means that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\in L^{\frac{4}{3}}(0,t;(\dot{W}^{1,2}(\Omega))^{\prime}).

Noting that
\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\in L^{2}(0,t;\dot{W}^{1,2}(\Omega))
, by Lemma
8.18
, we have

\displaystyle\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\rightarrow\nabla c^{\varepsilon}\quad{\rm in}\quad L^{2}(0,t;L^{2}(\Omega))

as
\tau\to 0.

Similar to Lemma
5.10
, we obtain

\displaystyle\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\rightarrow\nabla c^{\varepsilon}\quad{\rm in}\quad L^{q}(0,t;L^{q}(\Omega))\quad{\rm for}\quad q\in[2,\frac{10}{3})

as
\tau\to 0.

Lemma 5.12
.

\nabla\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\rightarrow\nabla\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon}}
in
L^{q}(0,t;L^{q}(\Omega)
) strongly for
q\in[2,\frac{10}{3})
as
\tau\to 0
.

Proof.
Consider the equation of
\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}
as follows:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}+u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot\nabla\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}-\frac{|\nabla\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}|^{2}}{\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}}-\Delta\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}=-\frac{\kappa(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})}{2\tau\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}}~~\ln(1+\tau n^{\varepsilon,\tau}).

Taking the derivative of the above equation, we have

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\nabla\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}+\nabla\left(u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot\nabla\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\right)-\nabla\frac{|\nabla\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}|^{2}}{\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}}-\Delta\nabla\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\tau}\nabla\left(\frac{\kappa(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})}{\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}}~~\ln(1+\tau n^{\varepsilon,\tau})\right).

Now for any smooth function
\zeta\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega\times(0,t))
, we know

\displaystyle\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}\partial_{t}\nabla\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\cdot\zeta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot\nabla\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\nabla\cdot\zeta-\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}\nabla^{2}\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}:\nabla\zeta

\displaystyle+~\frac{1}{2\tau}\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}\frac{\kappa(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})-\kappa(0)}{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\ln(1+\tau n^{\varepsilon,\tau})\nabla\cdot\zeta-\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}\frac{|\nabla\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}|^{2}}{\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}}\nabla\cdot\zeta

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\big|\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot\nabla\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\nabla\cdot\zeta\big|+\big|\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}\nabla^{2}\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}:\nabla\zeta\big|

\displaystyle+~\frac{1}{2\tau}\big|\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}\frac{\kappa(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})-\kappa(0)}{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}(\tau n^{\varepsilon,\tau})\nabla\cdot\zeta\big|+\big|\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}\frac{|\nabla\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}|^{2}}{\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}}\nabla\cdot\zeta\big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\frac{8}{3}}(0,t;L^{4}(\Omega))}\|\nabla\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\|_{L^{\frac{8}{3}}(0,t;L^{4}(\Omega))}\|\nabla\zeta\|_{L^{4}(0,t;L^{2}(\Omega))}

\displaystyle+~\|\nabla^{2}\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\|_{L^{2}(0,t;L^{2}(\Omega))}\|\nabla\zeta\|_{L^{2}(0,t;L^{2}(\Omega))}

\displaystyle+~C\|\kappa^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\|c^{\varepsilon}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}})}\|c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,t;L^{\infty}(\Omega))}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\frac{4}{3}}(0,t;L^{2}(\Omega))}\|\nabla\zeta\|_{L^{4}(0,t;L^{2}(\Omega))}

\displaystyle+~\|\frac{|\nabla\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}|^{2}}{\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}}\|_{L^{2}(0,t;L^{2}(\Omega))}\|\nabla\zeta\|_{L^{2}(0,t;L^{2}(\Omega))}.

Using (
3.5
) and interpolation inequality, we have

\displaystyle\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\frac{4}{3}}(0,t;L^{2}(\Omega))}=\|\sqrt{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\|_{L^{\frac{8}{3}}(0,t;L^{4}(\Omega))}\leq C.

By (
2.12
), (
5.2
), (
5.3
) -(
5.5
) and

\kappa(s)\in C^{2}(\overline{\mathbb{R^{+}}})
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}\partial_{t}\nabla\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\cdot\zeta

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left(\|\nabla\zeta\|_{L^{2}(0,t;L^{2}(\Omega))}+\|\nabla\zeta\|_{L^{4}(0,t;L^{2}(\Omega))}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|\nabla\zeta\|_{L^{4}(0,t;L^{2}(\Omega))},

which means that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\nabla\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\in L^{\frac{4}{3}}(0,t;(\dot{W}^{1,2}(\Omega))^{\prime}).

Noting that
\nabla\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\in L^{2}(0,t;\dot{W}^{1,2}(\Omega))
, by Lemma
8.18
, we have

\displaystyle\nabla\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\rightarrow\nabla\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon}}\quad{\rm in}\quad L^{2}(0,t;L^{2}(\Omega))

as
\tau\to 0.

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\nabla\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\rightarrow\nabla\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon}}\quad{\rm in}\quad L^{q}(0,t;L^{q}(\Omega))\quad{\rm for}\quad q\in[2,\frac{10}{3})

as
\tau\to 0.

Lemma 5.13
.

n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\rightarrow n^{\varepsilon}
strongly in
L^{q}(0,t;L^{q}(\Omega))
for
q\in[1,\frac{5}{3})
as
\tau\to 0
.

Proof.
For any smooth function
\zeta\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega\times(0,t))
, we know

\displaystyle\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}\partial_{t}n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot\zeta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left((u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\right)\cdot\zeta+\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}\Delta n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot\zeta

\displaystyle-\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}\nabla\cdot\left(\frac{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}}{1+\tau n^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\chi(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})\right)\cdot\zeta

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\frac{10}{3}}(0,t;L^{\frac{10}{3}}(\Omega))}\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\frac{5}{3}}(0,t;L^{\frac{5}{3}}(\Omega))}\|\nabla\zeta\|_{L^{10}(0,t;L^{10}(\Omega))}

\displaystyle+C\|\sqrt{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\|_{L^{\frac{10}{3}}(0,t;L^{\frac{10}{3}}(\Omega))}\|\nabla\sqrt{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\|_{L^{2}(0,t;L^{2}(\Omega))}\|\nabla\zeta\|_{L^{5}(0,t;L^{5}(\Omega))}

\displaystyle+C\|c_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\chi\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\|c_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}})}\|\nabla\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\|_{L^{\frac{10}{3}}(0,t;L^{\frac{10}{3}}(\Omega))}\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\frac{5}{3}}(0,t;L^{\frac{5}{3}}(\Omega))}\|\nabla\zeta\|_{L^{10}(0,t;L^{10}(\Omega))}

By uniform estimate (
3.5
), we have the boundedness of
\|u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\frac{10}{3}}(0,t;L^{\frac{10}{3}}(\Omega))}
,

\|\nabla\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\|_{L^{\frac{10}{3}}(0,t;L^{\frac{10}{3}}(\Omega))}
and
\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\frac{5}{3}}(0,t;L^{\frac{5}{3}}(\Omega))}
, thus

\displaystyle\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}\partial_{t}n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot\zeta\leq C\|\nabla\zeta\|_{L^{10}(0,t;L^{10}(\Omega))}.

It follows that
\partial_{t}n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\in L^{\frac{10}{9}}(0,t;(\dot{W}^{1,10}(\Omega))^{\prime}).

Using Hölder inequality, we get

\displaystyle\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|\nabla n^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{\frac{5}{4}}

\displaystyle=~\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|\sqrt{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\nabla\sqrt{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}}|^{\frac{5}{4}}

\displaystyle\leq~\left(\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|\nabla\sqrt{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{5}{8}}\left(\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{\frac{5}{3}}\right)^{\frac{3}{8}}\leq C.

We choose
X=W^{1,\frac{5}{4}}(\Omega)
,
Y=L^{2}{(\Omega)}
and
Z=W^{-1,\frac{10}{9}}(\Omega)
,
p=\frac{5}{4}
,
r=\frac{10}{9}
.
By Lemma
8.18
, we have

\displaystyle n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\rightarrow n^{\varepsilon}\quad L^{\frac{5}{4}}(0,t;L^{2}(\Omega)).

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\rightarrow n^{\varepsilon}\quad{\rm in}\quad L^{q}(0,t;L^{q}(\Omega))\quad{\rm for}\quad q\in[1,\frac{5}{3})

as
\tau\to 0.

Lemma 5.14
.

(n^{\varepsilon,\tau},c^{\varepsilon,\tau},u^{\varepsilon,\tau})
is a weak solution to the Cauchy problem (
1
), there holds

\displaystyle n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ln n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\in L^{\frac{3}{2}}(0,t;L^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega)).

Proof.
Set
Q=(0,t)\times\Omega
,
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{3}
is an arbitrary bounded domain, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ln n^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{\frac{3}{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{Q}|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ln n^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{\frac{3}{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{Q\cap\{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}>e\}}|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ln n^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{\frac{3}{2}}+\int_{Q\cap\{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\leq e\}}|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ln n^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{\frac{3}{2}}.

Since

\displaystyle\lim_{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\rightarrow\infty}{(n^{\varepsilon,\tau})}^{-\frac{1}{6}}|\ln n^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{\frac{3}{2}}=0,

we have

\displaystyle\int_{Q\cap\{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}>e\}}|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ln n^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{\frac{3}{2}}\leq C\int_{Q}|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{\frac{5}{3}}.

Noting that
n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\in L^{\infty}(0,t;L^{1}(\Omega))
and
\nabla\sqrt{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\in L^{2}(0,t;L^{2}(\Omega))
, we have

\displaystyle\|\sqrt{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\|_{L^{\frac{10}{3}}(0,t;L^{\frac{10}{3}}(\Omega))}\leq C,

which means

\displaystyle\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\frac{5}{3}}(0,t;L^{\frac{5}{3}}(\Omega))}\leq C.

Noting that

\displaystyle\lim_{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\rightarrow 0}(n^{\varepsilon,\tau})^{\frac{1}{6}}\ln n^{\varepsilon,\tau}=0,

we have

\displaystyle\int_{Q\cap\{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\leq e\}}|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ln n^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{\frac{3}{2}}\leq C\int_{Q}|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{\frac{4}{3}}\leq C|Q|^{\frac{1}{5}}\left(\int_{Q}|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{\frac{5}{3}}\right)^{\frac{4}{5}}\leq C.

It follows that

\displaystyle n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ln n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\in L^{\frac{3}{2}}(0,t;L^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega)).

Lemma 5.15
.

n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ln n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\rightarrow n^{\varepsilon}\ln n^{\varepsilon}
strongly in
L^{q}(0,t;L^{q}(\Omega))
for
q\in[1,\frac{3}{2})
as
\tau\to 0
.

Proof.
Firstly, we divide the following inequality into four parts as follows. We denote
Q=(0,t)\times\Omega
for simplicity.

\displaystyle\int_{Q}|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ln n^{\varepsilon,\tau}-n^{\varepsilon}\ln n^{\varepsilon}|dxdt

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}<1\}\bigcap\{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}>n^{\varepsilon}\}\bigcap Q}|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ln n^{\varepsilon,\tau}-n^{\varepsilon}\ln n^{\varepsilon}|

\displaystyle+\int_{\{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}<1\}\bigcap\{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}<n^{\varepsilon}\}\bigcap Q}|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ln n^{\varepsilon,\tau}-n^{\varepsilon}\ln n^{\varepsilon}|

\displaystyle+\int_{\{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}>1\}\bigcap Q}|(n^{\varepsilon,\tau}-n^{\varepsilon})\ln n^{\varepsilon,\tau}|+\int_{\{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}>1\}\bigcap Q}|n^{\varepsilon}(\ln n^{\varepsilon,\tau}-\ln n^{\varepsilon})|

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle N_{1}+N_{2}+N_{3}+N_{4}.

For the term of
N_{1}
, we arrive

\displaystyle N_{1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{\{n^{\varepsilon}<n^{\varepsilon,\tau}<1\}\bigcap Q}|(n^{\varepsilon,\tau}-n^{\varepsilon})\ln n^{\varepsilon,\tau}|+C\int_{\{n^{\varepsilon}<n^{\varepsilon,\tau}<1\}\bigcap Q}|n^{\varepsilon}\ln(\frac{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}}{n^{\varepsilon}})|

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle N_{11}+N_{12}.

For
N_{11}
, note that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow 0}n^{\frac{1}{9}}\ln n=0,

(5.7)

then by Hölder inequality, (
5.7
) and Lemma
5.13
, we get

\displaystyle N_{11}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\{n^{\varepsilon}<n^{\varepsilon,\tau}<1\}\bigcap Q}|(n^{\varepsilon,\tau}-n^{\varepsilon})(n^{\varepsilon,\tau})^{-\frac{1}{9}}|

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\{n^{\varepsilon}<n^{\varepsilon,\tau}<1\}\bigcap Q}|(n^{\varepsilon,\tau}-n^{\varepsilon})(n^{\varepsilon,\tau}-n^{\varepsilon})^{-\frac{1}{9}}|

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\{n^{\varepsilon}<n^{\varepsilon,\tau}<1\}\bigcap Q}|(n^{\varepsilon,\tau}-n^{\varepsilon})^{\frac{8}{9}}|

\displaystyle\leq C|Q|^{\frac{1}{9}}\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}-n^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{1}(Q)}\rightarrow 0\quad{\rm as}\quad\tau\rightarrow 0.

As for
N_{12}
, by inequality
\ln(1+f)\leq f
when
f>-1
and Lemma
5.13
, we have

\displaystyle N_{12}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\{n^{\varepsilon}<n^{\varepsilon,\tau}<1\}\bigcap Q}|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}-n^{\varepsilon}|\rightarrow 0\quad{\rm as}\quad\tau\rightarrow 0.

For the term of
N_{2},
we have

\displaystyle N_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{\{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}<1\}\bigcap\{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}<n^{\varepsilon}\}\bigcap Q}|(n^{\varepsilon}-n^{\varepsilon,\tau})\ln n^{\varepsilon}|

\displaystyle+C\int_{\{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}<1\}\bigcap\{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}<n^{\varepsilon}\}\bigcap Q}|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ln(\frac{n^{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{\{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}<1\}\bigcap\{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}<n^{\varepsilon}\}\bigcap Q\bigcap\{n^{\varepsilon}<1\}}|(n^{\varepsilon}-n^{\varepsilon,\tau})\ln n^{\varepsilon}|

\displaystyle+C\int_{\{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}<1\}\bigcap\{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}<n^{\varepsilon}\}\bigcap Q\bigcap\{n^{\varepsilon}\geq 1\}}|(n^{\varepsilon}-n^{\varepsilon,\tau})\ln n^{\varepsilon}|

\displaystyle+C\int_{\{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}<1\}\bigcap\{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}<n^{\varepsilon}\}\bigcap Q}|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ln(\frac{n^{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}})|

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle N_{21}+N_{22}+N_{23}.

For
N_{21}
, by (
5.7
) and Lemma
5.13
, we obtain

\displaystyle N_{21}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}<n^{\varepsilon}\}\bigcap Q}|(n^{\varepsilon}-n^{\varepsilon,\tau})^{\frac{8}{9}}|

\displaystyle\leq C|Q|^{\frac{1}{9}}\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}-n^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{1}(Q)}\rightarrow 0\quad{\rm as}\quad\tau\rightarrow 0.

Note that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}n^{-\frac{1}{6}}|\ln n|^{\frac{3}{2}}=0,

(5.8)

there holds

\displaystyle N_{22}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}<1\}\bigcap Q}|n^{\varepsilon}-n^{\varepsilon,\tau}||n^{\varepsilon}|^{\frac{1}{9}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}-n^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\frac{9}{8}}(Q)}\|n^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{1}(Q)}^{\frac{1}{9}}\rightarrow 0\quad{\rm as}\quad\tau\rightarrow 0.

Similarly, for the term of
N_{23}
, there holds

\displaystyle N_{23}\leq\int_{\{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}<1\}\bigcap\{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}<n^{\varepsilon}\}\bigcap Q}|n^{\varepsilon}-n^{\varepsilon,\tau}|\rightarrow 0\quad{\rm as}\quad\tau\rightarrow 0.

For the term of
N_{3}
,
by Hölder inequality, (
3.5
) , (
5.8
) and Lemma
5.13
, we arrive

\displaystyle N_{3}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}>1\}\bigcap Q}|(n^{\varepsilon,\tau}-n^{\varepsilon})\ln n^{\varepsilon,\tau}|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}>1\}\bigcap Q}|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}-n^{\varepsilon}||n^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{\frac{1}{9}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}-n^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\frac{9}{8}}(Q)}\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{1}(Q)}^{\frac{1}{9}}\rightarrow 0\quad{\rm as}\quad\tau\rightarrow 0.

Moreover, we get

\displaystyle N_{4}

\displaystyle=\int_{\{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}>1\}\bigcap Q}|n^{\varepsilon}\ln(\frac{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}-n^{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon}}+1)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}>1\}\bigcap Q}|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}-n^{\varepsilon}|\rightarrow 0\quad{\rm as}\quad\tau\rightarrow 0.

Collecting
N_{1}-N_{4}
, which follows that
n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ln n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\rightarrow n^{\varepsilon}\ln n^{\varepsilon}~~{\rm in}~~L^{1}(0,t;L^{1}(\Omega))
as
\tau\to 0.

Furthermore, we can obtain

\displaystyle n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ln n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\rightarrow n^{\varepsilon}\ln n^{\varepsilon}\quad{\rm in}\quad L^{q}(0,t;L^{q}(\Omega))\quad{\rm for}\quad q\in[1,\frac{3}{2})

as
\tau\to 0.

6.
The convergence of local energy inequality

We will show that
(u^{\varepsilon,\tau},c^{\varepsilon,\tau},n^{\varepsilon,\tau})
has a limit
(u^{\varepsilon},c^{\varepsilon},n^{\varepsilon})
satisfying local energy inequality
(
4.8
).

For the left part of (
4.8
), using properties of weak convergence, we see that

\displaystyle\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|\nabla\sqrt{n^{\varepsilon}}|^{2}\psi\leq\liminf_{\tau\to 0}\left(\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|\nabla\sqrt{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}}|^{2}\psi\right);

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}(|\nabla\sqrt{{c^{\varepsilon}}}|^{2}\psi)(\cdot,t)\leq\liminf_{\tau\to 0}\int_{\Omega}(|\nabla\sqrt{{c}^{\varepsilon,\tau}}|^{2}\psi)(\cdot,t);

\displaystyle\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|\Delta\sqrt{{c^{\varepsilon}}}|^{2}\psi\leq\liminf_{\tau\to 0}\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|\Delta\sqrt{{c}^{\varepsilon,\tau}}|^{2}\psi;

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|u^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\psi\leq\liminf_{\tau\to 0}\int_{\Omega}|u^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{2}\psi;

\displaystyle\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|\nabla u^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\psi\leq\liminf_{\tau\to 0}\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{2}\psi.

and

\displaystyle\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}(\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon}})^{-2}|\nabla\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon}}|^{4}\psi\leq\liminf_{\tau\to 0}\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}(\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}})^{-2}|\nabla\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}|^{4}\psi.

Noting that
n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ln n^{\varepsilon,\tau}>-e^{-1}
and the domain
\Omega
is bounded, by Fatou’s Lemma we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}(n^{\varepsilon}\ln n^{\varepsilon}\psi+2e^{-1}\psi)

\displaystyle\leq\liminf_{\tau\to 0}\int_{\Omega}(n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ln n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\psi+2e^{-1}\psi)dx,

that is

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}n^{\varepsilon}\ln n^{\varepsilon}\psi\leq\liminf_{\tau\to 0}\int_{\Omega}n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ln n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\psi,

where we used
\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ln n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{1}}\leq C(t)
, which means that uniformly bounded sequences have spot-convergent subcolumns.

For the right part of (
4.8
), by a priori estimate, Lemma
5.10
-Lemma 5.13, we have

\displaystyle u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\rightarrow u^{\varepsilon}\quad{\rm in}~~L^{p}((0,t);L^{p}(\Omega)),~~p\in[2,\frac{10}{3})~~{\rm as}~~\tau\rightarrow 0;

\displaystyle\nabla{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\rightarrow\nabla{c^{\varepsilon}}\quad{\rm in}~~L^{p}((0,t);L^{p}(\Omega)),~~p\in[2,\frac{10}{3})~~{\rm as}~~\tau\rightarrow 0;

\displaystyle\nabla\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\rightarrow\nabla\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon}}\quad{\rm in}~~L^{p}((0,t);L^{p}(\Omega)),~~p\in[2,\frac{10}{3})~~{\rm as}~~\tau\rightarrow 0;

\displaystyle n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\rightarrow n^{\varepsilon}\quad{\rm in}~~L^{p}((0,t);L^{p}(\Omega)),~~p\in[2,\frac{10}{3})~~{\rm as}~~\tau\rightarrow 0;

\displaystyle n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ln n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\rightarrow n^{\varepsilon}\ln n^{\varepsilon}\quad{\rm in}~~L^{p}((0,t);L^{p}(\Omega)),~~p\in[1,\frac{3}{2})~~{\rm as}~~\tau\rightarrow 0.

We also need the weak convergence of
P^{\varepsilon,\tau}
. By the equation of
u^{\varepsilon,\tau}
, we have

\displaystyle-\Delta(P^{\varepsilon,\tau}-\bar{P}^{\varepsilon,\tau})=\partial_{i}\partial_{j}((u^{\varepsilon,\tau}_{i}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})u^{\varepsilon,\tau}_{j})+\nabla\cdot((n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\nabla\phi)\ast\rho^{\varepsilon}).

By Calderón-Zygmund theorem, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|P^{\varepsilon,\tau}-\bar{P}^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{\frac{5}{3}}\leq C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|(u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\otimes u^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{\frac{5}{3}}+C\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|(n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\nabla\phi)\ast\rho^{\varepsilon}|^{\frac{15}{14}}\right)^{\frac{14}{15}}.

(6.1)

Using Hölder’s inequality and Young’s inequality, and noting that the strong convergence of convolution, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|(u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\otimes u^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{\frac{5}{3}}\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|u^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{\frac{10}{3}},

and

\displaystyle\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|(n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\nabla\phi)\ast\rho^{\varepsilon}|^{\frac{15}{14}}\right)^{\frac{14}{15}}\leq\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{\frac{15}{14}}\right)^{\frac{14}{15}}.

Integrating both sides of (
6.1
) with respect of
t
from
0
to
t
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|P^{\varepsilon,\tau}-\bar{P}^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{\frac{5}{3}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|u^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{\frac{10}{3}}+C\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{0}^{t}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{\frac{15}{14}}\right)^{\frac{14}{15}}

(6.2)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle I+II.

For
I,
by interpolation inequality, we have

I\leq C\left(\|u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,t;L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))}+\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{2}(0,t;L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))}\right)^{\frac{10}{3}}.

For
II,
noting that
n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\geq 0
, by Hölder’s and interpolation inequalities, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{\frac{15}{14}}dx\right)^{\frac{14}{15}}dt

(6.3)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(\sqrt{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\right)^{\frac{15}{7}}dx\right)^{\frac{14}{15}}dt

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle t^{\frac{9}{10}}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(\sqrt{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\right)^{\frac{15}{7}}dx\right)^{\frac{28}{3}}dt\right)^{\frac{1}{10}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle Ct^{\frac{9}{10}}\left(\|\sqrt{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,t;L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))}^{2}+\|\nabla\sqrt{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\|_{L^{2}(0,t;L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))}^{2}\right).

Then from (
3.5
),
we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|P^{\varepsilon,\tau}-\bar{P}^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{\frac{5}{3}}\leq C(t,\|n_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{1}},\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}),

which means

\displaystyle P^{\varepsilon,\tau}-\bar{P}^{\varepsilon,\tau}\rightharpoonup P^{\varepsilon}-\bar{P}^{\varepsilon}~~{\rm in}~~L^{\frac{3}{2}}(0,t;L^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega)),~~{\rm as}~~\tau\rightarrow 0.

Next, we prove the convegence of the right terms of the local energy inequality (
4.8
). Let us call the right terms of the local energy inequality (
4.8
) as
M_{1}
,
M_{2}
,
\cdots
,
M_{10}
term by term.

For
M_{1}
:

Since
n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ln n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\rightarrow n^{\varepsilon}\ln n^{\varepsilon}~~{\rm in}~~L^{\frac{10}{7}}((0,t);L^{\frac{10}{7}}(\Omega))~~{\rm as}~~\tau\rightarrow 0
,
and
\psi\in C_{c}^{\infty}
, we have
\partial_{t}\psi+\Delta\psi\in C_{c}^{\infty}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ln n^{\varepsilon,\tau}(\partial_{t}\psi+\Delta\psi)\rightarrow\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}n^{\varepsilon}\ln n^{\varepsilon}(\partial_{t}\psi+\Delta\psi)~~{\rm as}~~\tau\rightarrow 0.

For
M_{2}
:

\displaystyle\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ln n^{\varepsilon,\tau}(u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla\psi-\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}n^{\varepsilon}\ln n^{\varepsilon}(u^{\varepsilon}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla\psi

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ln n^{\varepsilon,\tau}[(u^{\varepsilon,\tau}-u^{\varepsilon})\ast\rho^{\varepsilon}]\cdot\nabla\psi

\displaystyle+\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}(n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ln n^{\varepsilon,\tau}-n^{\varepsilon}\ln n^{\varepsilon})(u^{\varepsilon}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla\psi

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ln n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\frac{3}{2}}(0,t;L^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega))}\|u^{\varepsilon,\tau}-u^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{3}(0,t;L^{3}(\Omega_{1}))}\|\nabla\psi\|_{L^{\infty}(0,t;L^{\infty}(\Omega))}

\displaystyle+\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ln n^{\varepsilon,\tau}-n^{\varepsilon}\ln n^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\frac{10}{7}}(0,t;L^{\frac{10}{7}}(\Omega))}\|u^{\varepsilon}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\frac{10}{3}}(0,t;L^{\frac{10}{3}}(\Omega_{1}))}\|\nabla\psi\|_{L^{\infty}(0,t;L^{\infty}(\Omega))}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle M_{21}+M_{22},

where
\Omega_{1}=\bigcup_{x\in\Omega}B_{1}(x)
.
Since
u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\rightarrow u^{\varepsilon}
in
L^{3}(0,t;L^{3}(\Omega_{1}))
, we have
M_{21}\to 0
as
\tau\rightarrow 0
.
By strong convergence of
n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ln n^{\varepsilon,\tau}
, we have
M_{22}\to 0~~{\rm as}~~\tau\rightarrow 0
.

For
M_{3}
:

For the term

\displaystyle M_{3}=\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}\left(\frac{n^{\varepsilon,\tau}}{1+\tau n^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\chi(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})\right)\ln n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot\nabla\psi,

we rewrite
M_{3}
as follow:

\displaystyle M_{3}-\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}n^{\varepsilon}\ln n^{\varepsilon}\nabla c^{\varepsilon}\chi(c^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla\psi

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}\left(\frac{1}{1+\tau n^{\varepsilon,\tau}}-1\right)n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ln n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\chi(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})\cdot\nabla\psi

\displaystyle+~\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}\left(n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ln n^{\varepsilon,\tau}-n^{\varepsilon}\ln n^{\varepsilon}\right)\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\chi(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})\cdot\nabla\psi

\displaystyle+~\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}n^{\varepsilon}\ln n^{\varepsilon}(\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\chi(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})-\nabla c^{\varepsilon}\chi(c^{\varepsilon}))\cdot\nabla\psi

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle M_{31}+M_{32}+M_{33}.

For
M_{31},
note that

\displaystyle\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}\left|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ln n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\chi(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})\cdot\nabla\psi\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ln n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\frac{3}{2}}(0,t;L^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega))}\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{3}(0,t;L^{3}(\Omega))}\|\chi\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\|c_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}})}\|\nabla\psi\|_{L^{\infty}(0,t;L^{\infty}(\Omega))}\leq~C,

and

\frac{1}{1+\tau n^{\varepsilon,\tau}}-1\to 0~~{\rm as}~~\tau\to 0,

which implies
M_{31}\to 0
as
\tau\to 0.

For
M_{32}
, by the strong convergence of
n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ln n^{\varepsilon,\tau}
,

\displaystyle M_{32}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|(n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ln n^{\varepsilon,\tau}-n^{\varepsilon}\ln n^{\varepsilon})\|_{L^{\frac{10}{7}}(0,t;L^{\frac{10}{7}}(\Omega))}\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{\frac{10}{3}}(0,t;L^{\frac{10}{3}}(\Omega))}

\displaystyle\cdot\|\chi\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\|c_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}})}\|\nabla\psi\|_{L^{\infty}(0,t;L^{\infty}(\Omega))}

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle 0~~{\rm as}~~\tau\to 0.

For
M_{33}
, we estimate it as follow:

\displaystyle M_{33}\leq

\displaystyle\|n^{\varepsilon}\ln n^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\frac{3}{2}}(0,t;L^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega))}\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\chi(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})-\nabla c^{\varepsilon}\chi(c^{\varepsilon})\|_{L^{3}(0,t;L^{3}(\Omega))}\|\nabla\psi\|_{L^{\infty}(0,t;L^{\infty}(\Omega))}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|n^{\varepsilon}\ln n^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\frac{3}{2}}(0,t;L^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega))}\|(\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}-\nabla c^{\varepsilon})\chi(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})\|_{L^{3}(0,t;L^{3}(\Omega))}\|\nabla\psi\|_{L^{\infty}(0,t;L^{\infty}(\Omega))}

\displaystyle+~\|n^{\varepsilon}\ln n^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\frac{3}{2}}(0,t;L^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega))}\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon}(\chi(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})-\chi(c^{\varepsilon}))\|_{L^{3}(0,t;L^{3}(\Omega))}\|\nabla\psi\|_{L^{\infty}(0,t;L^{\infty}(\Omega))}

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle M_{33}^{{}^{\prime}}+M_{33}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}.

For the first term of
M_{33}^{{}^{\prime}}
, by the continuity of function
\chi
, Hölder inequality and the strong convergence of
\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}
, we arrive

\displaystyle M_{33}^{{}^{\prime}}\leq

\displaystyle\|n^{\varepsilon}\ln n^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\frac{3}{2}}(0,t;L^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega))}\|\chi(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,t;L^{\infty}(\Omega))}\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}-\nabla c^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{3}(0,t;L^{3}(\Omega))}\|\nabla\psi\|_{L^{\infty}(0,t;L^{\infty}(\Omega))}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|n^{\varepsilon}\ln n^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\frac{3}{2}}(0,t;L^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega))}\|\chi\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\|c^{\varepsilon}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)})}\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}-\nabla c^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{3}(0,t;L^{3}(\Omega))}\|\nabla\psi\|_{L^{\infty}(0,t;L^{\infty}(\Omega))}

\displaystyle\rightarrow 0~~{\rm as}~~\tau\to 0.

For
M_{33}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}
, by Hölder inequality and mean value theorem, for
\theta\in(0,1)
, we achieve

\displaystyle M_{33}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}\leq

\displaystyle\|n^{\varepsilon}\ln n^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\frac{3}{2}}(0,t;L^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega))}\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon}(\chi(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})-\chi(c^{\varepsilon}))\|_{L^{3}(0,t;L^{3}(\Omega))}\|\nabla\psi\|_{L^{\infty}(0,t;L^{\infty}(\Omega))}

(6.4)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|n^{\varepsilon}\ln n^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\frac{3}{2}}(0,t;L^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega))}\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\frac{10}{3}}(0,t;L^{\frac{10}{3}}(\Omega))}

\displaystyle\cdot\|\chi^{{}^{\prime}}(\theta c^{\varepsilon,\tau}+(1-\theta)c^{\varepsilon})(c^{\varepsilon,\tau}-c^{\varepsilon})\|_{L^{30}(0,t;L^{30}(\Omega))}\|\nabla\psi\|_{L^{\infty}(0,t;L^{\infty}(\Omega))}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|n^{\varepsilon}\ln n^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\frac{3}{2}}(0,t;L^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega))}\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\frac{10}{3}}(0,t;L^{\frac{10}{3}}(\Omega))}\|\chi^{{}^{\prime}}(\theta c^{\varepsilon,\tau}+(1-\theta)c^{\varepsilon})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,t;L^{\infty}(\Omega))}

\displaystyle\cdot\|c^{\varepsilon,\tau}-c^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{30}(0,t;L^{30}(\Omega))}\|\nabla\psi\|_{L^{\infty}(0,t;L^{\infty}(\Omega))}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|n^{\varepsilon}\ln n^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\frac{3}{2}}(0,t;L^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega))}\|\nabla c^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\frac{10}{3}}(0,t;L^{\frac{10}{3}}(\Omega))}\|\chi\|_{L^{\infty}(0,2\|c^{\varepsilon}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)})}

\displaystyle\cdot\|c^{\varepsilon,\tau}-c^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{30}(0,t;L^{30}(\Omega))}\|\nabla\psi\|_{L^{\infty}(0,t;L^{\infty}(\Omega))}.

Using embedding inequality

\displaystyle\|c^{\varepsilon,\tau}-c^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{30}(0,t;L^{30}(\Omega))}=

\displaystyle\max|c^{\varepsilon,\tau}-c^{\varepsilon}|^{\frac{4}{5}}\left(\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|c^{\varepsilon,\tau}-c^{\varepsilon}|^{6}\right)^{\frac{1}{30}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|c^{\varepsilon,\tau}-c^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{4}{5}}\|\nabla(c^{\varepsilon,\tau}-c^{\varepsilon})\|_{L^{6}(0,t;L^{2}(\Omega))}^{\frac{1}{5}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|2c^{\varepsilon}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{4}{5}}\|\nabla(c^{\varepsilon,\tau}-c^{\varepsilon})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,t;L^{2}(\Omega))}^{\frac{2}{15}}\|\nabla(c^{\varepsilon,\tau}-c^{\varepsilon})\|_{L^{2}(0,t;L^{2}(\Omega))}^{\frac{1}{15}}.

Combining (
6.4
) and (
6
), by the strong convergence of
\nabla c^{\varepsilon,\tau}
,
we have
M_{33}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}\to 0~~{\rm as}~~\tau\rightarrow 0
.
To sum up,
M_{33}\to 0~~{\rm as}~~\tau\rightarrow 0
.
Collecting
M_{31}
to
M_{33}
, we have

\displaystyle M_{3}\to\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}n^{\varepsilon}\ln n^{\varepsilon}\nabla c^{\varepsilon}\chi(c^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla\psi\quad{\rm as}\quad\tau\to 0.

For
M_{4}
:
The analysis of
M_{4}
is similar to
M_{3},
we omit it. Ultimately, one can obtain

\displaystyle M_{4}\to\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}n^{\varepsilon}\nabla c^{\varepsilon}\chi(c^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla\psi\quad{\rm as}\quad\tau\to 0.

For
M_{5}
:

Since
\psi\in C_{c}^{\infty}
, and

\displaystyle\nabla\sqrt{{c}^{\varepsilon,\tau}}\rightarrow\nabla\sqrt{{c^{\varepsilon}}}\quad{\rm in}~~L^{p}(0,t;L^{p}(\Omega)),~~p\in[2,\frac{10}{3})~~{\rm as}~~\tau\rightarrow 0.

Then

\displaystyle\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|\nabla\sqrt{{c}^{\varepsilon,\tau}}|^{2}(\partial_{t}\psi+\Delta\psi)-\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|\nabla\sqrt{{c^{\varepsilon}}}|^{2}(\partial_{t}\psi+\Delta\psi)\rightarrow 0~~{\rm as}~~\tau\rightarrow 0.

For
M_{6}
:

\displaystyle\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|\nabla\sqrt{{c}^{\varepsilon,\tau}}|^{2}{(u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})}\cdot\nabla\psi-\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|\nabla\sqrt{{c^{\varepsilon}}}|^{2}({u^{\varepsilon}}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla\psi

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|\nabla\sqrt{{c}^{\varepsilon,\tau}}|^{2}[(u^{\varepsilon,\tau}-u^{\varepsilon})\ast\rho^{\varepsilon}]\cdot\nabla\psi+\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}(|\nabla\sqrt{{c}^{\varepsilon,\tau}}|^{2}-|\nabla\sqrt{{c^{\varepsilon}}}|^{2})(u^{\varepsilon}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla\psi

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\nabla\sqrt{{c^{\varepsilon,\tau}}}\|^{2}_{L^{3}(0,t;L^{3}(\Omega))}\|u^{\varepsilon,\tau}-u^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{3}(0,t;L^{3}(\Omega))}\|\nabla\psi\|_{L^{\infty}(0,t;L^{\infty}(\Omega))}

\displaystyle+~\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}(|\nabla\sqrt{{c}^{\varepsilon,\tau}}|^{2}-|\nabla\sqrt{{c^{\varepsilon}}}|^{2})(u^{\varepsilon}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla\psi

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle M_{61}+M_{62}.

Similar to
M_{2}
, by
u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\to u^{\varepsilon}
in
L^{3}(0,t;L^{3}(\Omega))
and the strong convergence of
\nabla\sqrt{{c}^{\varepsilon,\tau}}
, we get

\displaystyle\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|\nabla\sqrt{{c}^{\varepsilon,\tau}}|^{2}{(u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})}\cdot\nabla\psi\rightarrow\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|\nabla\sqrt{{c^{\varepsilon}}}|^{2}({u^{\varepsilon}}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla\psi~~{\rm as}~~\tau\rightarrow 0.

For
M_{7}
:

Since
\psi\in C_{c}^{\infty}
, we have
\partial_{t}\psi+\Delta\psi\in C_{c}^{\infty}
and

\displaystyle u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\to u^{\varepsilon}\quad{\rm in}\quad L^{3}(0,t;L^{3}(\Omega))~~{\rm as}~~\tau\rightarrow 0.

By the definition of strong convergence, we have

\displaystyle\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|u^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{2}\left(\partial_{t}\psi+\Delta\psi\right)-\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|u^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\left(\partial_{t}\psi+\Delta\psi\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(t,|\Omega|)||u^{\varepsilon,\tau}-u^{\varepsilon}||^{2}_{L^{3}(0,t;L^{3}(\Omega))}\rightarrow 0~~{\rm as}~~\tau\rightarrow 0.

For
M_{8}
:

\displaystyle\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|u^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{2}(u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla\phi-\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|u^{\varepsilon}|^{2}(u^{\varepsilon}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla\phi

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|u^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{2}[(u^{\varepsilon,\tau}-u^{\varepsilon})\ast\rho^{\varepsilon}]\cdot\nabla\phi+\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}(|u^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{2}-|u^{\varepsilon}|^{2})(u^{\varepsilon}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla\phi

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^{3}(0,t;L^{3}(\Omega))}^{2}||(u^{\varepsilon,\tau}-u^{\varepsilon})\ast\rho^{\varepsilon}||_{L^{3}(0,t;L^{3}(\Omega_{1}))}\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^{\infty}(0,t;L^{\infty}(\Omega))}

\displaystyle+~\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}(|u^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{2}-|u^{\varepsilon}|^{2})(u^{\varepsilon}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla\phi.

By the convergence property of convolution,
u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\to u^{\varepsilon}
in
L^{3}(0,t;L^{3}(\Omega)),
and the strong convergence of
|u^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{2}
, we can achieve

\displaystyle\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|u^{\varepsilon,\tau}|^{2}(u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla\phi\rightarrow\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|u^{\varepsilon}|^{2}(u^{\varepsilon}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla\phi

as
\tau\to 0.

For
M_{9}
:

\displaystyle\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}(P^{\varepsilon,\tau}-\bar{P}^{\varepsilon,\tau})u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot\nabla\psi-\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}(P^{\varepsilon}-\bar{P}^{\varepsilon})u^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla\psi

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}(P^{\varepsilon,\tau}-\bar{P}^{\varepsilon,\tau})(u^{\varepsilon,\tau}-u^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla\psi+\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}[(P^{\varepsilon,\tau}-\bar{P}^{\varepsilon,\tau})-(P^{\varepsilon}-\bar{P}^{\varepsilon})]u^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla\psi

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle||P^{\varepsilon,\tau}-\bar{P^{\varepsilon,\tau}}||_{L^{\frac{3}{2}}(0,t;L^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega))}||u^{\varepsilon,\tau}-u^{\varepsilon}||_{L^{3}(0,t;L^{3}(\Omega))}\|\nabla\psi\|_{L^{\infty}(0,t;L^{\infty}(\Omega))}

\displaystyle+~\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}[(P^{\varepsilon,\tau}-\bar{P}^{\varepsilon,\tau})-(P^{\varepsilon}-\bar{P}^{\varepsilon})]u^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla\psi.

Since

\displaystyle u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\to u^{\varepsilon}\quad{\rm in}\quad L^{3}(0,t;L^{3}(\Omega))~~{\rm as}~~\tau\rightarrow 0.

Then

\displaystyle||P^{\varepsilon,\tau}-\bar{P}^{\varepsilon,\tau}||_{L^{\frac{3}{2}}(0,t;L^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega))}||u^{\varepsilon,\tau}-u^{\varepsilon}||_{L^{3}(0,t;L^{3}(\Omega))}\|\nabla\psi\|_{L^{\infty}}\rightarrow 0~~{\rm as}~~\tau\rightarrow 0.

Since
|u^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla\psi|\leq C|\nabla\psi||u^{\varepsilon}|\in L^{3}(0,t;L^{3}(\Omega))
, by the the definition of weak convergence, we have

\displaystyle\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}[(P^{\varepsilon,\tau}-\bar{P}^{\varepsilon,\tau})-(P^{\varepsilon}-\bar{P}^{\varepsilon})]u^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla\psi\rightarrow 0~~{\rm as}~~\tau\rightarrow 0.

For
M_{10}
:

\displaystyle\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}(n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\nabla\phi)\ast\rho^{\varepsilon}\cdot u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\psi-\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}(n^{\varepsilon}\nabla\phi)\ast\rho^{\varepsilon}\cdot u^{\varepsilon}\psi

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}[(n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\nabla\phi-n^{\varepsilon}\nabla\phi)\ast\rho^{\varepsilon}]\cdot u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\psi+\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}((n^{\varepsilon}\nabla\phi)\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot(u^{\varepsilon,\tau}-u^{\varepsilon})\psi

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}(n^{\varepsilon,\tau}-n^{\varepsilon})\nabla\phi\cdot(u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\psi)\ast\rho^{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle+~\|n^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\frac{3}{2}}(0,t;L^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega))}||u^{\varepsilon,\tau}-u^{\varepsilon}||_{L^{3}(0,t;L^{3}(\Omega))}||\psi||_{L^{\infty}(0,t;L^{\infty}(\Omega))}\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^{\infty}(0,t;L^{\infty}(\Omega))}.

Since

\displaystyle n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\rightarrow n^{\varepsilon}\quad{\rm in}~~L^{\frac{3}{2}}(0,t;L^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega))~~{\rm as}~~\tau\rightarrow 0,

and

|\nabla\phi\cdot(u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\psi)\ast\rho^{\varepsilon}|\in L^{3}(0,t;L^{3}(\Omega_{1})).

Using strong convergence of
n^{\varepsilon,\tau}
, we get

\displaystyle\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}(n^{\varepsilon,\tau}\nabla\phi-n^{\varepsilon}\nabla\phi)\ast\rho^{\varepsilon}\cdot u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\psi\rightarrow 0~~{\rm as}~~\tau\rightarrow 0.

Combining with
u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\to u^{\varepsilon}
in
L^{3}(0,t;L^{3}(\Omega))
as
\tau\rightarrow 0
, we have

M_{10}\to\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}(n^{\varepsilon}\nabla\phi)\ast\rho^{\varepsilon}\cdot u^{\varepsilon}\psi~~{\rm as}~~\tau\rightarrow 0.

To sum up, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}(n^{\varepsilon}\ln(n^{\varepsilon})\psi)(\cdot,t)+4\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|\nabla\sqrt{n^{\varepsilon}}|^{2}\psi+\frac{2}{\Theta_{0}}\int_{\Omega}(|\nabla\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon}}|^{2}\psi)(\cdot,t)

(6.5)

\displaystyle+\frac{4}{3\Theta_{0}}\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|\Delta\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon}}|^{2}\psi+\frac{2}{3\Theta_{0}}\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}(\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon}})^{-2}|\nabla\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon}}|^{4}\psi

\displaystyle+\frac{18}{\Theta_{0}}\|{c^{\varepsilon}_{0}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{\Omega}(|u^{\varepsilon}|^{2})(\cdot,t)\psi+\frac{18}{\Theta_{0}}\|{c^{\varepsilon}_{0}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|\nabla u^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\psi

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}n^{\varepsilon}\ln n^{\varepsilon}(\partial_{t}\psi+\Delta\psi)+\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}n^{\varepsilon}\ln n^{\varepsilon}(u^{\varepsilon}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla\psi

\displaystyle+\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}n^{\varepsilon}\chi(c^{\varepsilon})\nabla c^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla\psi+\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}n^{\varepsilon}\ln n^{\varepsilon}\chi(c^{\varepsilon})\nabla c^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla\psi

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{\Theta_{0}}\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|\nabla\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon}}|^{2}(\partial_{t}\psi+\Delta\psi)+\frac{2}{\Theta_{0}}\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|\nabla\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon}}|^{2}(u^{\varepsilon}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla\psi

\displaystyle+\frac{18}{\Theta_{0}}||c^{\varepsilon}_{0}||_{L^{\infty}}\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|u^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\left(\partial_{t}\psi+\Delta\psi\right)+\frac{18\mu}{\Theta_{0}}||c^{\varepsilon}_{0}||_{L^{\infty}}\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|u^{\varepsilon}|^{2}(u^{\varepsilon}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla\psi

\displaystyle+\frac{36}{\Theta_{0}}||c^{\varepsilon}_{0}||_{L^{\infty}}\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}(P^{\varepsilon}-\bar{P}^{\varepsilon})u^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla\psi-\frac{36}{\Theta_{0}}||c^{\varepsilon}_{0}||_{L^{\infty}}\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}(n^{\varepsilon}\nabla\phi)\ast\rho^{\varepsilon}\cdot u^{\varepsilon}\psi.

7.
proof of Theorem
1.2

Proof.

By convergence, we know that
(u^{\varepsilon},c^{\varepsilon},n^{\varepsilon})
satisfies the equation as follows:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{llll}\displaystyle\partial_{t}n^{\varepsilon}+(u^{\varepsilon}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla n^{\varepsilon}-\Delta n^{\varepsilon}=-\nabla\cdot\left(n^{\varepsilon}\nabla c^{\varepsilon}\chi(c^{\varepsilon})\right),\\
\displaystyle\partial_{t}c^{\varepsilon}+u^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla c^{\varepsilon}-\Delta c^{\varepsilon}=-n^{\varepsilon}\kappa(c^{\varepsilon}),\\
\displaystyle\partial_{t}u^{\varepsilon}+\mu(u^{\varepsilon}\ast\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla u^{\varepsilon}-\Delta u^{\varepsilon}+\nabla P^{\varepsilon}=-(n^{\varepsilon}\nabla\phi)\ast\rho^{\varepsilon},\\
\displaystyle\nabla\cdot u^{\varepsilon}=0,\\
\displaystyle n^{\varepsilon}(x,0)=n^{\varepsilon}_{0}(x),\quad c^{\varepsilon}(x,0)=c^{\varepsilon}_{0}(x),\quad u^{\varepsilon}(x,0)=u^{\varepsilon}_{0}(x).\\
\end{array}\right.

By completely similar arguments as the convergence of
(u^{\varepsilon,\tau},c^{\varepsilon,\tau},n^{\varepsilon,\tau})
in the subsection, we can get the convergence of
(u^{\varepsilon},c^{\varepsilon},n^{\varepsilon}).
We omitted it. Thereby,
(u,c,n)
satisfies the following local energy inequality:

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}(n\ln(n)\psi)(\cdot,t)+4\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|\nabla\sqrt{n}|^{2}\psi+\frac{2}{\Theta_{0}}\int_{\Omega}(|\nabla\sqrt{c}|^{2}\psi)(\cdot,t)

(7.7)

\displaystyle+\frac{4}{3\Theta_{0}}\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|\Delta\sqrt{c}|^{2}\psi+\frac{18}{\Theta_{0}}\|{c_{0}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{\Omega}(|u|^{2})(\cdot,t)\psi

\displaystyle+\frac{18}{\Theta_{0}}\|{c_{0}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|\nabla u|^{2}\psi+\frac{2}{3\Theta_{0}}\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}(\sqrt{c})^{-2}|\nabla\sqrt{c}|^{4}\psi

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}n\ln(n)(\partial_{t}\psi+\Delta\psi)+\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}n\ln(n)u\cdot\nabla\psi

\displaystyle+\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}n\chi(c)\nabla c\cdot\nabla\psi+\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}n\ln n\chi(c)\nabla c\cdot\nabla\psi

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{\Theta_{0}}\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|\nabla\sqrt{c}|^{2}(\partial_{t}\psi+\Delta\psi)+\frac{2}{\Theta_{0}}\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|\nabla\sqrt{c}|^{2}u\cdot\nabla\psi

\displaystyle+\frac{18}{\Theta_{0}}||c_{0}||_{L^{\infty}}\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|u|^{2}\left(\partial_{t}\psi+\Delta\psi\right)+\frac{18\mu}{\Theta_{0}}||c_{0}||_{L^{\infty}}\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}|u|^{2}u\cdot\nabla\psi

\displaystyle+\frac{36}{\Theta_{0}}||c_{0}||_{L^{\infty}}\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}(P-\bar{P})u\cdot\nabla\psi-\frac{36}{\Theta_{0}}||c_{0}||_{L^{\infty}}\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega}n\nabla\phi\cdot u\psi.

Thus the third condition of Definition
1.1
about suitable weak solution is proved.

To sum up, the proof of Theorem
1.2
is complete.

8.
Appendix

Lemma 8.16

(See Theorem A.1 in
[
1
]
)
.

Let
X
be a Banach space, let
B\subset X
be a closed bounded subset, and let
\Phi:B\to B
be Lipschitz continuous in the norm topology with Lipschitz constant
L<1;
i.e.,
\|\Phi(x)-\Phi(y)\|\leq L\|x-y\|
and
L<1.
Then
\Phi
has a unique fixed point in
B.

Lemma 8.17

(See
[
1
]
Proposition A.16.)
.

\nu
is a nonzero constant,
n
is the dimension of space, the operator
e^{\nu t\Delta}
satisfies the following a priori estimate for
1\leq q\leq p\leq\infty
and
0\leq s\leq r<\infty

\|e^{\nu t\Delta}f\|_{\dot{W}^{r,p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\leq C(r,s)(\nu t)^{\frac{n}{2}(\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{q})-\frac{(r-s)}{2}}\|f\|_{\dot{W}^{s,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})},

as soon as
t>0.

Lemma 8.18

(See
[
30
]
Lemma 3.7)
.

Let
X\subset\subset Y\subset Z
be reflexive Banach spaces, if
0<T<\infty
,
1\leq p,1<r
,
f\in L^{p}(0,T;X)
satisfy

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\|f(t)\|_{X}^{p}dt\leq C_{1},\quad\int_{0}^{T}\|\frac{d}{dt}f(t)\|_{Z}^{r}dt\leq C_{2}.

for some constants
C_{1},C_{2}<\infty
, then
{f}_{j},j\in\mathbb{N}
is relatively compact in
L^{p}(0,T;Y)
.

Lemma 8.19

(See
[
16
]
Theorem 2.4)
.

Let
\alpha\in(0,d)
,
d
is dimension, then
\frac{1}{(\sqrt{-\Delta})^{\alpha}}
is bounded:

(1)

from
L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
to
L^{\frac{d}{d-\alpha},\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
;

(2)

from
L^{\frac{d}{\alpha},1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
to
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
;

(3)

from
L^{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
to
L^{p_{1},q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
for
1<p<\frac{d}{\alpha}
and
\frac{1}{p_{1}}=\frac{1}{p}-\frac{\alpha}{d}
.
